Nevertheless, some remarks can be added for the computation. The Bianchi identities lead to two equalities
Eq. 3.20
and
Eq. 3.21
. Using the Dragon theorem
[
26
]
, it can be shown that the first relations automatically imply the validity of the seconds, meaning that we can only consider
Eq. 3.20
. Moreover, the conversion of spin indices to vector indices is also used intensively. For example, the following relation for the curvature tensor is valid:

R_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\gamma\dot{\gamma}\delta\dot{\delta}}=\sigma^{\mu}_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\sigma^{\nu}_{\delta\dot{\delta}}R_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\mu\nu}=2\epsilon_{\gamma\delta}R_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}-2\epsilon_{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}R_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\gamma\delta}\ ,

where the second equality is obtained by decomposing the tensor in irreducible representations of
SL(2,\mathbb{C})=\overline{SO(1,3)}
. To be more precise, we first denote,

\displaystyle R_{MN\alpha}{}^{\beta}=\frac{1}{4}R_{MN\mu\nu}\left(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\right)_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\ ,\ R_{MN}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}=\frac{1}{4}R_{MN\mu\nu}\left(\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\sigma^{\nu}\right)^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\ .

(3.24)

Since
R_{MN\mu\nu}
is skew-symmetric by exchange of the indices
(\mu,\nu)
(see
Eq. 3.18
), we can then write:

\displaystyle R_{MN\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(R_{MN\mu\nu}-R_{MN\nu\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\sigma_{\mu}\right)^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}R_{MN}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}-\left(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\right)_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}R_{MN\alpha}{}^{\beta}\right)\ .

By expanding the elements of the curvature tensor in spinor indices as the first equality of
Eq. 3.24
we then get

\displaystyle R_{MN\gamma\dot{\gamma}\delta\dot{\delta}}=2\epsilon_{\gamma\delta}R_{MN\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}-2\epsilon_{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}R_{MN\gamma\delta}\ .

Decomposing the elements
R_{MN\gamma\dot{\gamma}\delta\dot{\delta}}
into irreducible representations of
SL(2,\mathbb{C})
:

\displaystyle R_{MN\gamma\dot{\gamma}\delta\dot{\delta}}=\epsilon_{\gamma\delta}w_{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}+\epsilon_{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}w^{\prime}_{\gamma\delta}

where the tensors
w
and
w^{\prime}
can be easily obtained through
Eq. 3.24
.

Finally, we get:

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}J^{\delta\gamma}\ ,

(3.25)

\displaystyle\Big\{\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\beta}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}R_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}J^{\dot{\delta}\dot{\gamma}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}{}^{\mu}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\gamma}J^{\gamma\beta}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\alpha\mu}{}^{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{T}_{\alpha\mu}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha\mu\beta\gamma}J^{\gamma\beta}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\alpha\mu\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}}J^{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\beta}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Big[\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}},\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\dot{\alpha}\mu}{}^{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{T}_{\dot{\alpha}\mu}{}^{\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\dot{\alpha}\mu\beta\gamma}J{\gamma\beta}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\dot{\alpha}\mu\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}}J^{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\beta}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{D}_{\mu},\mathcal{D}_{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{T}_{\mu\nu}^{\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\mu\nu\beta\gamma}J^{\gamma\beta}-\frac{1}{2}R_{\mu\nu\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}}J^{\dot{\gamma}\dot{\beta}}\ .

3.2.3
Invariance of supergravity under Weyl supergroup

The algebra
Eq. 3.25
is invariant under superdiffeomorphisms
Eq. 3.13
and local Lorentz transformations
Eq. 3.12
. However, there exists a larger group of transformations of
J^{MN}
and
\mathcal{D}_{M}
, which leaves the graded commutator
Eq. 3.17
invariant. Indeed, the automorphism group of
Eq. 3.25
is the super-Weyl group, also called superconformal group (which is equivalent to the Weyl group for general relativity)
[
27
]
[
28
]
. The aim of this section is to identify the superconformal variations
\delta_{\Sigma}J^{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}
and
\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{D}_{M}
.

Using the well-known commutation relations of the generators
J^{MN}
:

\Big[J^{MN},J^{PQ}\Big]=i\left(-\eta^{NP}J^{MQ}+\eta^{PM}J^{NQ}-\eta^{NQ}J^{PM}+\eta^{QM}J^{PN}\right)\ ,

with
\eta^{MN}=\left(\eta^{\mu\nu},\epsilon^{\alpha\beta},\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\right)
, the equality
\delta J^{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=0
follows immediately. For the transformation of the covariant derivative, we define:

\delta\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}=-\Phi\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}+\left(\Phi_{\alpha}\right)^{\beta\gamma}J_{\beta\gamma}

where
\Phi
and
(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\beta\gamma}
are arbitrary superfields (with
(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\beta\gamma}=(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\gamma\beta}
).

Making a transformation of the anti-commutator
\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}
gives:

\displaystyle\delta\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Phi\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}-\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Phi\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}-\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\Phi\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\gamma\delta}\left[\epsilon_{\delta\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}+\epsilon_{\gamma\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\delta}\right]

(3.26)

\displaystyle-(\Phi_{\beta})^{\gamma\delta}\left[\epsilon_{\delta\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}+\epsilon_{\gamma\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\delta}\right]+\mathcal{D}_{\beta}(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\gamma\delta}J_{\delta\gamma}+\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}(\Phi_{\beta})^{\gamma\delta}J_{\delta\gamma}

The structure
Eq. 3.25
is invariant under super-Weyl transformations. The variation
3.26
must then be expressed only with Lorentz generators in the left-handed representation. Imposing that the other terms vanish leads to

\displaystyle(\Phi_{\alpha})^{\gamma\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[\delta^{\beta}{}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}^{\gamma}\Phi+\delta^{\gamma}{}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}^{\beta}\Phi\right]\ ,

and thus we obtain

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}=-\Phi\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}+\mathcal{D}^{\gamma}\Phi J_{\alpha\gamma}\ .

Similarly, we can deduce
\delta\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}
. The variation
\delta\mathcal{D}_{\mu}
is obtained from the development of
\delta\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right\}
with the help of
Eq. 3.25
. Defining the chiral superfield
\Sigma
and introducing
\Phi=2\Sigma^{\dagger}-\Sigma
, we get:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\Sigma-2\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}-\mathcal{D}^{\gamma}\Sigma J_{\alpha\gamma}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\Sigma^{\dagger}-2\Sigma\Big]\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\gamma}}\Sigma^{\dagger}J_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\gamma}}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{\mu}-\frac{i}{2}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{D}}\Sigma^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}\mathcal{D}+\mathcal{D}\Sigma\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}\bar{\mathcal{D}}\Big]-\mathcal{D}^{\nu}\Big(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big)J_{\mu\nu}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}J_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}J_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\ .

From the expressions of the non-vanishing torsion components
Eq. 3.23
and
Eq. 3.25
, the variation of the three superfields
\mathcal{R}
,
G_{\mu}
and
W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}
can also be calculated. Using the variation
3.26
:

\displaystyle\delta\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}=\left[\left(2\Sigma^{\dagger}-\Sigma\right)R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha\delta}\epsilon_{\beta\gamma}\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Sigma+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\beta\delta}\epsilon_{\alpha\gamma}\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Sigma\right]J^{\delta\gamma}=-\frac{1}{2}\delta R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}J^{\delta\gamma}

with:

\displaystyle\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}\Sigma=0\quad,\quad\left[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right]=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Sigma\ ,

the transformation of
\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}
can be computed using the relation between the components of curvature tensor
R_{MNPQ}

[
6
]
[
7
]
:

\displaystyle R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=4\left(\epsilon_{\alpha\gamma}\epsilon_{\beta\delta}+\epsilon_{\beta\gamma}\epsilon_{\alpha\delta}\right)\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\ .

(3.27)

Those calculations give:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\Sigma\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{4}\Big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\Big)\Sigma^{\dagger}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}G_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]G_{\mu}+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Big[\Sigma-\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3\Sigma W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}\ .

Transformation laws of the superconnection can also be deduced by developing the covariant derivatives as
Eq. 3.15
. Lengthy calculus give:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Omega_{\tilde{M}\beta\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{M}\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\Sigma+E_{\tilde{M}\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Sigma-E_{\tilde{M}\gamma}\epsilon_{\gamma\alpha}(\sigma^{\nu\rho})_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}\Big[\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Omega_{\tilde{M}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{M}\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\gamma}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+E_{\tilde{M}\dot{\gamma}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\beta}}\Sigma^{\dagger}-E_{\tilde{M}\nu}\epsilon_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\alpha}}(\bar{\sigma}^{\nu\rho})^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}\Big[\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Omega_{\tilde{M}\nu\rho}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E_{\tilde{M}\nu}\mathcal{\rho}\Big[\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]+E_{\tilde{M}\rho}\mathcal{D}_{\nu}\Big[\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\Big]-2E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{\alpha}(\sigma_{\nu\rho})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Sigma-2E_{\tilde{M}\dot{\alpha}}(\bar{\sigma}_{\nu\rho})^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\beta}}\Sigma^{\dagger}\ .

Those results will be helpful in a future section (see
subsection 3.5.2
). Indeed, as we will see, the computation of the supergravity action will lead to non-correctly normalised kinetic terms. In order to rescale the Lagrangian in order to get correctly normalised kinetic terms, a Weyl-rescaling followed by a shift of the gravitino field (so-called
gravitino-shift
) will be performed.

3.2.4
Supergravity transformations and gauge fixing conditions

We will now investigate the transformations of the main superfields under local Lorentz transformations (
Eq. 3.12
) and superdiffeomorphisms (
Eq. 3.13
). Considering a vector superfield
V^{M}
, the supervierbein
E_{M}{}^{\tilde{M}}
and the superconnection
\Omega_{\tilde{M}P}{}^{Q}
, their transformations can be written as:

\displaystyle V^{M}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle V^{\prime}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z}^{\prime})=V^{N}(\mathfrak{z})\Lambda_{N}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})\ ,

(3.28)

\displaystyle\Omega_{\tilde{M}P}{}^{Q}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}_{\tilde{M}P}{}^{Q}(\mathfrak{z}^{\prime})=\frac{\partial\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{N}}}{\partial\mathfrak{z}^{\prime}{}^{\tilde{M}}}(\Lambda^{-1})_{P}{}^{R}(\mathfrak{z})\Big[\Omega_{\tilde{N}R}{}^{S}(\mathfrak{z})\Lambda_{S}{}^{Q}(\mathfrak{z})-\partial_{\tilde{N}}\Lambda_{R}{}^{Q}(\mathfrak{z})\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z}^{\prime})=\frac{\partial\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{N}}}{\partial\mathfrak{z}^{\prime}{}^{\tilde{M}}}E_{\tilde{N}}{}^{N}(\mathfrak{z})\Lambda_{N}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})\ .

Developing the parameters around the identity, the matrix
\Lambda_{M}{}^{N}(\mathfrak{z})
can be written as:

\Lambda_{N}{}^{M}=\delta_{N}{}^{M}+L_{N}{}^{M}\ .

Using
Eq. 3.28
we obtain:

\displaystyle\delta V^{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V^{\prime}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})-V{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\xi^{\tilde{P}}\partial_{\tilde{P}}V^{M}+V^{N}L_{N}{}^{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\xi^{\tilde{P}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{P}}V^{M}+V^{N}\left(\xi^{\tilde{P}}\Omega_{\tilde{P}N}{}^{M}+L_{N}{}^{M}\right)\ ,

where the relation can be simplified by redefining
L_{N}{}^{M}\rightarrow L^{\prime}{}_{N}{}^{M}=\xi^{\tilde{P}}\Omega_{\tilde{P}N}{}^{M}+L_{N}{}^{M}
. The variation of
G^{\mu}
can then be computed. In the same manner, we have:

\displaystyle\delta E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{M}}\xi^{M}+\xi^{\tilde{N}}\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{N}\tilde{M}}{}^{M}+E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{P}L_{P}{}^{M}\ ,

(3.29)

\displaystyle\delta\Omega_{\tilde{M}P}{}^{Q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\xi^{\tilde{N}}R_{\tilde{N}\tilde{M}P}{}^{Q}+\Omega_{\tilde{M}P}{}^{R}L_{R}{}^{Q}-L_{P}{}^{R}\Omega_{\tilde{M}R}{}^{Q}-\partial_{\tilde{M}}L_{P}{}^{Q}\ .

(3.30)

For the variation of the superfields
\mathcal{R}
, we obtain directly:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\xi^{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{R}\ .

(3.31)

We will see in the next sections that the development of the supergravity action is based on the lowest components in the
\theta
-expansion of the superfields. Recalling that
\xi^{M}(\mathfrak{z})
and
L_{M}{}^{N}
are superfields, they can be expanded in terms of Grassmann variables in the Einstein frame. For the superfield
\xi^{M}(\mathfrak{z})
defining the superdiffeomorphisms:

\displaystyle\xi^{M}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi^{(0,0)M}(x)+\theta^{\tilde{\alpha}}\xi_{\tilde{\alpha}}^{(1,0)M}(x)+\bar{\theta}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\xi^{(0,1)M}{}^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}(x)+\theta\sigma_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\theta}\xi^{(1,1)M}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}(x)

(3.32)

\displaystyle+\theta\cdot\theta\xi^{(2,0)M}(x)+\bar{\theta}\cdot\bar{\theta}\xi^{(0,2)M}(x)+\bar{\theta}\cdot\bar{\theta}\theta^{\tilde{\alpha}}\xi_{\tilde{\alpha}}^{(1,2)M}(x)

\displaystyle+\theta\cdot\theta\bar{\theta}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\xi^{(2,1)M}{}^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}(x)+\theta\cdot\theta\bar{\theta}\cdot\bar{\theta}\xi^{(2,2)M}(x)\ .

The same type of expansions can also be done for
L_{M}{}^{N}
. Using the large arbitrariness of the parameters, some components can be set to zero. Defining
X\big|=X\big|_{\theta=\bar{\theta}=0}
, we can fix, after analysing the variation
\delta\Omega_{MN}{}^{P}\big|
from
Eq. 3.30
:

\Omega_{\tilde{\mu}M}{}^{N}\big|=\omega_{\tilde{\mu}M}{}^{N}(x)\quad,\quad\Omega_{\tilde{\alpha}M}{}^{N}\big|=0\quad,\quad\Omega^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}_{M}{}^{N}\big|=0\ ,

(3.33)

where
\omega_{\tilde{\mu}M}{}^{N}=\left(\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\mu}{}^{\nu},\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\alpha}{}^{\beta},\omega_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\right)
is the spin-connection of general relativity. By examining the variation of the lowest components of the supervierbein
\delta E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}\big|
from
Eq. 3.29
, and taking into account the invertibility of
E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}
, a proper choice for the components of
\xi^{\tilde{M}}
in
Eq. 3.32
and for
L_{M}{}^{N}
allows to eliminate some components of
E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}\big|
:

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}(\mathfrak{z})\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}(x)&\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}(x)&\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\alpha}}(x)\\
0&\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\alpha}&0\\
0&0&\delta^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(3.34)

\displaystyle E_{M}{}^{\tilde{M}}(\mathfrak{z})\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}(x)&-\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}(x)&-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}(x)\\
0&\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}&0\\
0&0&\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(3.35)

where
e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}(x)
is the helicity-2 graviton field and
(\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha},\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\dot{\alpha}})
the Weyl components of the spin-
3/2
gravitino field. Since the relations
E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}E_{M}{}^{\tilde{N}}=\delta_{\tilde{M}}{}^{\tilde{N}}
and
E_{M}{}^{\tilde{M}}E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{N}=\delta_{M}{}^{N}
hold, we have:

\displaystyle e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}=\delta_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}\,

,

\displaystyle\,e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}=\delta_{\mu}{}^{\nu}\ ,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}=e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\,

,

\displaystyle\,\bar{\psi}_{\mu\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}=e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\alpha}}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\ ,

It can be shown that the lowest components of the superfields
\mathcal{R}
and
G_{\mu}
cannot be eliminated. We then define a complex scalar field
M(x)
and a real vector field
b_{\mu}
by:

\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=-\frac{1}{6}M(x)\quad,\quad G_{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=-\frac{1}{3}b_{\mu}(x).

We thus obtain the degrees of freedom of the supergravity multiplet that contains a helicity-2 field
e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}(x)
(the graviton), Weyl components
(\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}(x),\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x))
of the spin-
3/2
gravitino and two auxiliary fields
M(x)
and
b_{\mu}(x)
. The superconnection is not a fundamental field (see
subsection 3.2.5
), so it doesn’t belong to the multiplet. This multiplet is called the
minimal
supergravity multiplet. Other constraints can lead to different numbers of auxiliary fields (see
[
29
]
[
30
]
[
31
]
). This is out of the scope of this thesis.

The gauge fixing conditions (Equations
3.33
,
3.34
and
3.34
) are, nevertheless, not preserved by the general transformations
Eq. 3.28
. We must then specify the transformations which preserve the gauge previously defined. In a first step, we assume

\xi^{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=0\quad,\quad\xi^{\alpha}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=\epsilon^{\alpha}\quad,\quad\bar{\xi}_{\dot{\alpha}}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}\quad,\quad L_{MN}(\mathfrak{z})\big|=0\ .

Since
\delta E_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\mu}\big|=\delta E^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}\mu}\big|=0
, using
Eq. 3.29
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\delta E_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\tilde{\alpha}}\xi^{\mu}\big|+2i\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\alpha}\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\epsilon}^{\dot{\alpha}}=0\ ,

\displaystyle\delta E^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bar{\partial}^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\xi^{\mu}\big|-2i\delta^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\epsilon^{\alpha}\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}=0\ .

It is then possible to express the parameters
\xi^{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})
as:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})=2i\left(\theta^{\tilde{\alpha}}\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\alpha}\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\epsilon}^{\dot{\alpha}}+\epsilon^{\alpha}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\bar{\theta}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\delta^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right)+\cdots

where the dots correspond to other terms coming from higher-order components. In order to recover a parameter invariant under
3.28
, we can just make the substitution
\left(\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\alpha},\delta^{\dot{\tilde{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right)\rightarrow\left(E_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\alpha},E^{\dot{\tilde{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right)
leading to:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})=2i\Big[\tilde{\theta}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}-\epsilon\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\tilde{\theta}}\Big]

with
\tilde{\theta}_{\alpha}=\theta^{\tilde{\alpha}}E_{\tilde{\alpha}}{}^{\beta}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}
. The parameters
L_{MN}(\mathfrak{z})
associated with the transformations which preserve the gauge fixing constraints can be obtained using
\delta\Omega_{\tilde{\alpha}M}{}^{N}\big|=\delta\Omega^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{M}{}^{N}=0
. We finally obtain:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mu}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\Big[\bar{\theta}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}-\epsilon\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\theta}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\xi^{\alpha}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}_{\dot{\alpha}}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Big[\tilde{\theta}_{\alpha}(2\epsilon_{\beta}M^{*}+b_{\beta\dot{\gamma}}\bar{\epsilon}^{\dot{\gamma}})+\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}(2\epsilon_{\alpha}M^{*}+b_{\alpha\dot{\gamma}}\bar{\epsilon}^{\dot{\gamma}})\Big]\ ,

(3.36)

\displaystyle L_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Big[\tilde{\bar{\theta}}_{\dot{\alpha}}(2\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\beta}}M+\epsilon^{\gamma}b_{\gamma\dot{\beta}})+\tilde{\bar{\theta}}_{\dot{\beta}}(2\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}M+\epsilon^{\gamma}b_{\gamma\dot{\alpha}})\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle L_{\mu\nu}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big[(\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu})^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}L_{\dot{\alpha}}{}^{\dot{\beta}}+(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}L^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}\Big]\ .

3.2.5
Lowest order component fields

All those relations can now be used in order to calculate the lowest order components of the three fundamental superfields
\mathcal{R}
,
G_{\mu}
and
W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}
as well as their derivatives. This is a very lengthy computation for some derivatives of the fields. Therefore, only the method and the final results will be mentioned.

Since
\mathcal{R}
,
G_{\mu}
and
W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}
are related to the torsion tensor
\mathcal{T}_{MN}{}^{Q}
, the components
\mathcal{T}_{MN}{}^{Q}\big|
must be firstly computed. From
Eq. 3.19
and the lowest components of the supervierbein (
3.34
,
3.35
) and the superconnection
Eq. 3.33
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}+\partial_{\tilde{\nu}}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}-\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}+\omega_{\tilde{\nu}\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\ ,

(3.37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\nu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}\big)=-\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\alpha}}\big)=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

where we have introduced:

\displaystyle\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\nu}\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\nu}{}^{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\psi_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}=\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\nu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}\quad

,

\displaystyle\quad\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}=\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}=\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}+\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\beta}\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\beta}{}^{\alpha}\quad

,

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}=\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}+\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\beta}}\omega_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

The component
\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}\big|
can also be developed in flat indices using the supervierbein:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{M}E_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{N}\mathcal{T}_{MN}{}^{\mu}\big|

(3.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(E_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\gamma}E_{\tilde{\nu}\dot{\delta}}+E_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\delta}}E_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\gamma}\big)\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}{}^{\dot{\delta}\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}\big(\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}-\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\big)\ .

From
Eq. 3.37
,
Eq. 3.38
and symmetry property of the spin-connection, the expression of
\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\tilde{\rho}}=e_{\mu\tilde{\rho}}\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}
can be found:

\displaystyle\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\tilde{\rho}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}e_{\mu\tilde{\rho}}\big(\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}-\partial_{\tilde{\nu}}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\big)+\frac{1}{2}e_{\mu\tilde{\mu}}\big(\partial_{\tilde{\nu}}e_{\tilde{\rho}}{}^{\mu}-\partial_{\tilde{\rho}}e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}e_{\mu\tilde{\nu}}\big(\partial_{\tilde{\rho}}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}-\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\tilde{\rho}}{}^{\mu}\big)+\frac{i}{4}e_{\mu\tilde{\rho}}\big(\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}-\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{4}e_{\tilde{\mu}\mu}\big(\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\rho}}-\psi_{\tilde{\rho}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}\big)+\frac{i}{4}e_{\mu\tilde{\nu}}\big(\psi_{\tilde{\rho}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}-\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\rho}}\big)\ .

Thus, the spin-connection
\omega_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\tilde{\rho}}
is then not a fundamental field and can be written in terms of the graviton and the gravitino.

Thereafter, using the relations between the torsion components
\mathcal{T}_{MN}{}^{Q}
and the fundamental superfields
\mathcal{R}
,
G_{\mu}
and
W_{(\alpha\beta\gamma)}

Eq. 3.23
, it is then possible to evaluate their lowest components as well as their derivatives. In a similar way, the connection between those superfields and the components of the curvature tensor
R_{MNPQ}
is also used (see
[
7
]
[
6
]
).

The lowest components read:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}M\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}M^{*}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}(\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu})_{\alpha}+\frac{i}{6}(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu})_{\alpha}M-\frac{i}{6}\psi_{\mu\alpha}b^{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}(\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\nu})^{\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{i}{6}(\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\psi_{\mu})^{\dot{\alpha}}M^{*}+\frac{i}{6}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}b^{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{R}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\frac{4}{9}\left|M\right|^{2}-\frac{2}{9}b_{\mu}b^{\mu}-\frac{2i}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}b^{\mu}-\frac{1}{3}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\cdot\bar{\psi}^{\mu}M\ ,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\psi_{\nu}\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}b^{\mu}-\frac{2i}{3}\bar{\psi}^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\Big[\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}+\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\frac{4}{9}\left|M\right|^{2}-\frac{2}{9}b_{\mu}b^{\mu}+\frac{2i}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}b^{\mu}-\frac{1}{3}\psi_{\mu}\cdot\psi^{\mu}M^{*}\ ,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\psi_{\mu}\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\nu}b^{\nu}-\frac{2i}{3}\psi^{\mu}\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\Big[\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}+\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle G_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}b_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\delta}G_{\gamma\dot{\alpha}}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\psi}_{\delta}{}^{\dot{\epsilon}}{}_{\gamma\dot{\epsilon\dot{\alpha}}}-\frac{1}{12}\epsilon_{\delta\gamma}\bar{\psi}^{\epsilon\dot{\epsilon}}{}_{\epsilon\dot{\alpha}\dot{\epsilon}}+\frac{i}{6}\psi_{\gamma\dot{\alpha}\delta}M^{*}+\frac{i}{12}(\bar{\psi}_{\delta\dot{\epsilon}\dot{\alpha}}b_{\gamma}{}^{\dot{\epsilon}}+\bar{\psi}_{\gamma\dot{\epsilon}}{}^{\dot{\epsilon}}b_{\delta\dot{\alpha}}+\bar{\psi}_{\delta\dot{\epsilon}}{}^{\dot{\epsilon}}b_{\gamma\dot{\alpha}})\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\delta}}G_{\alpha\dot{\gamma}}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\psi^{\epsilon}{}_{\dot{\delta}\epsilon\dot{\gamma}\alpha}-\frac{1}{12}\epsilon_{\dot{\delta}\dot{\gamma}}\psi_{\alpha}{}^{\dot{\epsilon}\epsilon}{}_{\dot{\epsilon}\epsilon}-\frac{i}{6}\bar{\psi}_{\alpha\dot{\gamma}\dot{\delta}}M-\frac{i}{12}(\psi_{\epsilon\dot{\delta}\alpha}b^{\epsilon}{}_{\dot{\gamma}}+\psi_{\epsilon\dot{\gamma}}{}^{\epsilon}b_{\alpha\dot{\delta}}+\psi_{\epsilon\dot{\delta}}{}^{\epsilon}b_{\alpha\dot{\gamma}})\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\delta}G^{\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}(\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\nu}{}^{\mu})_{\delta}-\frac{i}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho})_{\delta}+\frac{i}{6}\psi^{\mu}{}_{\delta}M^{*}+\frac{i}{6}(\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\nu})_{\delta}b^{\mu}+\frac{1}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu})_{\delta}b_{\rho}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\delta}}G^{\mu}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}(\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\nu}{}^{\mu})^{\dot{\delta}}-\frac{i}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho})^{\dot{\delta}}-\frac{i}{6}\bar{\psi}^{\mu\dot{\delta}}M+\frac{i}{6}(\bar{\sigma}^{\nu\psi_{\nu}})^{\dot{\delta}}b^{\mu}-\frac{1}{12}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu})^{\dot{\delta}}b_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle W_{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{48}\sum_{r\in S_{3}}\left(\psi_{\alpha_{\tau(1)}\dot{\delta}\alpha_{\tau(2)}}{}^{\dot{\delta}}{}_{\alpha_{\tau(3)}}-i\psi_{\alpha_{\tau(1)}\dot{\delta}\alpha_{\tau(2)}}b_{\alpha_{\tau(3)}}{}^{\dot{\delta}}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{48}\sum_{r\in S_{3}}\left(\bar{\psi}_{\delta\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(1)}}{}^{\delta}{}_{\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(2)}\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(3)}}+i\bar{\psi}_{\delta\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(1)}\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(2)}}b^{\delta}{}_{\dot{\alpha}_{\tau(3)}}\right)\ .

with
S^{3}
the permutation group with three elements and:

\displaystyle G_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}G_{\mu}\ ,\ b_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}b_{\mu}\ ,\ \psi_{\beta\dot{\beta}\gamma\dot{\gamma}}{}^{\alpha}=\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\sigma^{\nu}{}_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{\beta\dot{\beta}\gamma\dot{\gamma}\dot{\alpha}}=\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\sigma^{\nu}{}_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}\dot{\alpha}}\ .

The supergravity transformation of the fields
e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}
,
\psi_{\mu}
,
M
and
b_{\mu}
can also be derived from the previous results. For example, the variation of the vierbein can be obtained from:

\displaystyle\delta e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}=\delta E_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\big|=-\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\xi^{\mu}\big|+\xi^{M}\mathcal{T}_{M\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\big|+E_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}L_{\nu}{}^{\mu}\big|\ .

Using
Eq. 3.19
, we easily find:

\displaystyle\delta e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}=-i\big[\epsilon(x)\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}-\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}(x)\big]\ .

(3.39)

The transformations of the other fields also follow using the same process:

\displaystyle\delta\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\epsilon^{\alpha}(x)+\frac{i}{3}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}\big(\bar{\epsilon}(x)\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\big)^{\alpha}M+\frac{i}{3}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}\big[\big(\epsilon(x)\sigma_{\rho}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\big)^{\alpha}b^{\rho}-3\epsilon^{\alpha}(x)b_{\gamma}\big]\ ,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}(x)+\frac{i}{3}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}\big(\epsilon(x)\sigma_{\nu}\big)_{\dot{\alpha}}M^{*}-\frac{i}{3}e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\nu}\big[\big(\bar{\epsilon}(x)\bar{\sigma}_{\rho}\sigma_{\nu}\big)_{\dot{\alpha}}b^{\rho}-3\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}(x)b_{\nu}\big]\ ,

\displaystyle\delta M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\epsilon\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu})+i(\epsilon\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu})M-i(\epsilon\cdot\psi_{\mu})b^{\mu}\ ,

(3.40)

\displaystyle\delta M^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\nu})+i(\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\psi_{\mu})M^{*}+i(\bar{\epsilon}\cdot\bar{\psi}_{\mu})b^{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta b^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\nu}{}^{\mu}-\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\nu}{}^{\mu}-\frac{i}{4}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\Big[\epsilon\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}+\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\Big]+\frac{i}{2}\Big[\epsilon\cdot\psi^{\mu}M^{*}-\bar{\epsilon}\cdot\bar{\psi}^{\mu}M\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\Big[\epsilon\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\nu}+\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\nu}\Big]b^{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\Big[\epsilon\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu}-\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu}\Big]b_{\rho}\ .

We clearly see that the fields
\big(e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu},\psi_{\tilde{\mu}},M,b_{\mu}\big)
form a multiplet under supergravity transformations as explained previously.

3.3
Superfield approach in curved superspace

There exist two types of superfields relevant to particle physics in the context of
N=1
supersymmetry theory: the chiral superfield
\Phi(\mathfrak{z})=\left(\phi,\chi,F\right)
containing a scalar field
\phi
, a Weyl spinor
\chi
and a scalar auxiliary field
F
and the vector superfield
V(\mathfrak{z})=\left(v_{\mu},\lambda,D\right)
with
v_{\mu}
a vector field,
\lambda
a Majorana spinor and
D
an auxiliary field.

As briefly explained in
subsection 3.2.2
, the definition of chiral superfields in supersymmetry does not generally extend in supergravity. However, imposing the proper torsion constraints such as
Eq. 3.22
allows to extend the notion of chiral superfields in supergravity.

In this section, the chiral superfields
\Phi
and the vector superfields
V
will be introduced. The components and derivatives of those superfields and their transformations according to supergravity transformations will be given. The interactions between those two superfields will also be derived.

3.3.1
Chiral superfields

In supersymmetry, chiral (anti-chiral) superfields
\Phi(\mathfrak{z}^{M})
(
\Phi^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{z}^{M})
) are defined through the constraints:

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Phi(\mathfrak{z}^{M})=0\quad,\quad D_{\alpha}\Phi^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{z}^{M})=0\ ,

(3.41)

where the
D_{\alpha}
and
\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}
are the superderivatives in supersymmetry. Following the torsion constraints
Eq. 3.22
, a similar definition can be extended in the context of supergravity. Modifying the superderivatives to covariant derivatives, we can then write:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Phi(\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{M}})=0\quad,\quad\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\Phi^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{M}})=0\ .

Note that although the superfield
\Phi(\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{M}})
depends on curved coordinates
\mathfrak{z}^{\tilde{M}}=\big(x^{\tilde{\mu}},\theta^{\tilde{\alpha}},\bar{\theta}_{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\big)
, its definition relies on flat indices.

In the same manner as
Eq. 3.32
, the chiral superfield
\Phi
can be expressed in supersymmetry using an expansion in
\theta
-variables. Even though this method is still possible in supergravity, it is more tedious since the expansion is done in the curved frame, which imposes to know all the components of the supervierbein
E_{\tilde{M}}{}^{M}
in
\theta
-expansion. It is then more suitable to define the components of the chiral superfield according to the (covariant) derivatives of the superfield:

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi\big|\quad,\quad\chi_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\Phi\big|\quad,\quad F=\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}^{\alpha}\Phi\big|\ ,

(3.42)

\displaystyle\phi^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{\dagger}\big|\quad,\quad\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\alpha}}\Phi^{\dagger}\big|\quad,\quad F^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Phi^{\dagger}\big|\ .

(3.43)

The calculation of the other lowest components of the derivatives of
\Phi
(up to the fourth derivatives) is also mandatory to fully expand the Lagrangian of supergravity. Since covariant derivatives are defined in the Einstein frame (see
Eq. 3.15
), the indices must firstly be changed to tilted indices where the gauge fixing conditions can be imposed (
3.33
3.34
3.35
). Only some examples of calculations will be shown. A complete presentation can be found in
[
6
]
[
7
]
.

Using
Eq. 3.42
,
Eq. 3.15
and
E_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\big|=e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}
, the covariant derivative
\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Phi
can be easily calculated:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Phi\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{M}}\Phi\big|=E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\Phi\big|+E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\alpha}}\Phi\big|+E_{\mu\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\Phi\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\big(\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\phi-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\cdot\chi\big)\equiv\hat{D}_{\mu}\phi\ ,

which defines the derivative covariant with respect to supergravity transformations.

The value of
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Phi\big|
directly follows the definition of the auxiliary field
F
in
Eq. 3.42
. Using the property
A_{\alpha}B_{\beta}-A_{\beta}B_{\alpha}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}A\cdot B
and the graded commutator
Eq. 3.17
with
Eq. 3.22
we get the two identities:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\right)\Phi\big|=2\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}F\quad,\quad\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}\Phi\big|=0\ ,

which gives
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\Phi\big|=2\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}F
.

The derivation of the third derivative
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\Phi\big|
leads to interesting results. Using the property:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}+\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}+\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}-\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}=0

and the vanishing commutator
\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}\Phi=0
(see
Eq. 3.17
), we can write:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\Phi=\frac{1}{3}\left(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}+\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}-\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}-\mathcal{D}_{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right)\ .

By contracting the last two indices using
\epsilon^{\beta\gamma}
and with
Eq. 3.27
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Phi=-\frac{2}{3}\left\{\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\mathcal{D}_{\beta}\right\}\mathcal{D}^{\beta}\Phi=8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\Phi

(3.44)

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\Big)\Phi=0

(3.45)

since
\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}
is an anti-chiral superfield,
i.e.
,
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}=0
. This is an important result. The equality
Eq. 3.45
means that the operator
\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}
acting on the chiral superfield
\Phi
generates an anti-chiral superfield. Hence, it is possible to associate a chiral (anti-chiral) superfield
\Xi
(
\Xi^{\dagger}
) to any anti-chiral (chiral) superfield
\Phi^{\dagger}
(
\Phi
) through the operator
\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}
(
\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}
):

\Xi=\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\Phi^{\dagger}\quad,\quad\Xi^{\dagger}=\left(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\right)\Phi\ .

This operator is the analogue of the operator
D\cdot D
in supersymmetry (where
D
is the superderivative in supersymmetry) and will be central to construct invariant action (given in Subsection
3.4.1
).

We now turn to the transformation of the chiral superfields under supergravity transformations. Using the definition of a supergravity transformation
Eq. 3.36
and the variation

\displaystyle\delta\Phi=-\xi^{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{M}}\Phi\ ,

the transformations of
\phi
,
\chi_{\alpha}
and
F
are:

\displaystyle\delta\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}\epsilon\cdot\chi\ ,

(3.46)

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\Big(\epsilon_{\alpha}F-i\big(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}\big)_{\alpha}\hat{D}_{\mu}\phi\Big)\ ,

(3.47)

\displaystyle\delta F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}\epsilon\cdot\chi M^{*}-i\sqrt{2}\hat{D}_{\mu}\chi\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{6}b_{\mu}\chi\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}\ .

(3.48)

Note that the variation of the auxiliary field
F
is not a total derivative (compared to what happens in supersymmetry).

3.3.2
Vector superfields

The vector superfield is defined in the same way as in supersymmetry by the reality condition

V=V^{\dagger}\ .

We suppose a non-abelian gauge group with the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and
V=V^{a}T_{a}
,
T_{a}
being the generators of the gauge group, in an appropriate matrix representation, taken to be hermitian. The vector superfield transforms then as:

e^{2gV}\rightarrow e^{-2ig\Lambda}e^{2gV}e^{2ig\Lambda^{\dagger}}\ ,

with the chiral superfield
\Lambda=\Lambda^{a}T_{a}
(where
a
corresponds to the index in the adjoint representation of
\mathfrak{g}
). Similarly than in supersymmetry, we adopt the Wess-Zumino gauge in order to eliminate all the non-physical fields
[
32
]
:

\displaystyle V\big|=0\quad,\quad\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\quad,\quad\mathcal{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}V\big|=0\ ,

(3.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}V\big|=0\quad

,

\displaystyle\quad\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}V\big|=0\ .

We also denote the strength tensor by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left[\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right]e^{2gV}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}e^{-2gV}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left[\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\right]e^{-2gV}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}e^{2gV}\ ,

(3.50)

with
g
, the coupling constant related to the defined gauge group. The various physical components of the vector superfield are defined using the covariant derivative:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha}=\frac{i}{2g}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\big|\quad,\quad\bar{\lambda}=\frac{-i}{2g}\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\big|\quad,\quad D^{a}=\frac{1}{4g}\mathcal{D}^{\alpha}\mathcal{W}^{a}_{\alpha}\big|=\frac{1}{4g}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\mathcal{W}}^{a\dot{\alpha}}\big|\ ,

(3.51)

\displaystyle v_{\mu}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}\big[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\big]V\big|\ ,

where
v_{\mu}
is a gauge boson,
(\lambda,\bar{\lambda})
a Majorana spinor (called the gaugino) and
D
an auxiliary field.

As explained in Section
3.3.1
, the calculus of the various derivatives of
V
and
\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
is required for the computation of the final supergravity action. Because the superfield strength tensor is a function of the vector superfield
V
, the derivatives of
V
must firstly be computed. The method is similar to the calculation with the chiral superfield. In the Wess-Zumino gauge
Eq. 3.49
and using the definition of the covariant derivative
Eq. 3.15
,
\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}V\big|
vanishes and so:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}V\big|=E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{M}V\big|=E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}V\big|+E_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\big|+E_{\mu\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\tilde{\dot{\alpha}}}V\big|=0\ .

The computation of the other derivatives is fully presented in
[
6
]
[
7
]
. We also have in the Wess-Zumino gauge (since
V^{3}=0
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\Big]e^{2gV}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}e^{-2gV}

(3.52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\Big]\Big(-2g\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V+2g^{2}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V^{2}-4g^{2}V\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V+\cdots\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g}{2}\Big[\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\Big]\Big(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V+g\big[V,\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\big]\Big)\ .

The various components of
\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
can then be computed starting from
Eq. 3.52
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2ig\lambda_{\alpha}\ ,

(3.53)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2ig\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\mathcal{W}_{\beta}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g\left[i(\sigma^{\mu\nu})_{\alpha\beta}(\hat{F}_{\mu\nu})+\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}D\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{\dot{\beta}}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2g\left[i(\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\nu})_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}(\hat{F}_{\mu\nu})+\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}D\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2g\left[4\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\hat{D}_{\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}-2ib_{\mu}(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\lambda})_{\alpha}-2iM^{*}\lambda_{\alpha}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2g\left[4\sigma^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\hat{D}_{\mu}\lambda^{\alpha}+2ib_{\mu}(\lambda\sigma^{\mu})_{\dot{\alpha}}+2iM\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right]\ ,

(3.54)

with:

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\mu}v_{\nu}-\hat{D}_{\nu}v_{\mu}+ig\left[v_{\mu},v_{\nu}\right]=e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}\hat{F}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}-\frac{i}{2}\left[\bar{\lambda}\bar{\sigma}_{\tilde{\nu}}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}+\lambda\sigma_{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}-\bar{\lambda}\bar{\sigma}_{\tilde{\mu}}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}-\lambda\sigma_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\mu}v_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}v_{\nu}-\frac{i}{2}\left[\bar{\lambda}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\psi_{\mu}-\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}\lambda\right]-\frac{i}{2}(\psi_{\nu}\sigma^{\rho}\bar{\psi}_{\mu})v_{\rho}\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\mu}\lambda_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\lambda_{\alpha}+ig\left[v_{\tilde{\mu}},\lambda_{\alpha}\right]\right)-\frac{1}{2}(\sigma^{\nu\rho}\psi_{\mu})_{\alpha}(\hat{F}_{\nu\rho})+\frac{i}{2}D\psi_{\mu\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\mu}\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}+ig\left[v_{\tilde{\mu}},\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right]\right)+\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu\rho})_{\dot{\alpha}}(\hat{F}_{\nu\rho})-\frac{i}{2}D\bar{\psi}_{\mu\dot{\alpha}}

and
F_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}=e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}=e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}e_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\nu}(\hat{D}_{\mu}v_{\nu}-\hat{D}_{\nu}v_{\mu})
. We now consider an infinitesimal supergravity transformation with parameters
\xi^{M}
. By checking the variation of the non-physical fields we get, for example:

\displaystyle\chi_{\alpha}\equiv\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\Big|=0\quad\rightarrow\quad\delta\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\Big|=-\xi^{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{M}}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}V\Big|=-(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon})_{\alpha}v_{\mu}\neq 0\ ,

meaning that supergravity transformations do not preserve the Wess-Zumino gauge. To remain in this gauge, we have to combine a supergravity transformation with a specific gauge transformation. Identifying the parameters of the gauge transformation which compensate the supergravity transformation, we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\delta v_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\big(\epsilon\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\lambda}-\lambda\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}\big)-\frac{i}{2}\big(\psi_{\mu}\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\epsilon}-\epsilon\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\big)v_{\nu}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\sigma^{\mu\nu}\epsilon\big)_{\alpha}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}+i\epsilon_{\alpha}D\ ,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\big(\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\nu}\big)_{\dot{\alpha}}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}-i\bar{\epsilon}_{\dot{\alpha}}D\ ,

\displaystyle\delta D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon\sigma^{\mu}\hat{D}\bar{\lambda}-\hat{D}_{\mu}\lambda\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}+\frac{i}{2}b_{\mu}\big(\epsilon\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\lambda}-\lambda\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\epsilon}\big)+\frac{i}{2}\big(M^{*}\epsilon\cdot\lambda-M\bar{\epsilon}\cdot\bar{\lambda}\big)\ .

Similarly, we also have to specify the variation of the chiral supermultiplets
\big(\phi,\chi_{\alpha},F\big)
. Those transformations can be found in
[
6
]
[
7
]
.

3.3.3
Gauge interactions of chiral superfields

Chiral superfields transform under gauge transformations as
\Phi\rightarrow e^{-2ig\Lambda}\Phi
. Terms like
\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV}\Phi
are then gauge invariant, as in supersymmetry. The gauge invariant kinetic term in supergravity can then be obtained through the couplings
\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV}
and
e^{-2gV}\Phi
. We then introduce (anti-)chiral superfield using the "projector" presented in Subsection
3.3.1
:

\mathcal{X}=\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV}\quad,\quad\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}=\left(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\right)e^{-2gV}\Phi\ .

In the same manner as for the strength tensor
\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
in the Wess-Zumino gauge (where
V^{3}=0
)
Eq. 3.52
, the superfields
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}
reduce to:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}=\Xi-2g\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\Phi^{\dagger}V+2g^{2}\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\Phi^{\dagger}V^{2}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}=\Xi^{\dagger}-2g\left(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\right)V\Phi+2g^{2}\left(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}\right)V^{2}\Phi\ .

Calculation of all of the components of
\mathcal{X}
can be found in
[
6
]
[
7
]
.

3.4
Construction of the action in curved superspace

The superfields are now defined in the context of curved superspace. There is, however, still some aspects that must be analysed for constructing the supergravity action. The chiral density and new
\Theta
-variables allowing to simplify the computations will be introduced. We will then be able to construct a compact form of the supergravity Lagrangian invariant under a combination of Super-Weyl and Kähler transformations.

3.4.1
Chiral density and new hybrid variables
\big(\Theta^{\alpha},\bar{\Theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\big)

As we have seen in
Eq. 3.42
and
Eq. 3.43
, the components of superfields are defined through derivatives with flat indices, whereas the expansion involves variables in the Einstein frame. This point leads to some difficulties in the development. It is then convenient to define a new set of variables
\xi^{\overline{M}}=\big(x^{\tilde{\mu}},\Theta^{\alpha},\bar{\Theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\big)
such as components of chiral superfields are defined as their derivatives. We have then for the
\Phi
-expansion:

\displaystyle\Phi(x,\Theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi(x,\theta)\Big|+\Theta\cdot\big(\mathcal{D}\Phi(x,\theta)\big)\Big|-\frac{1}{4}\Theta\cdot\Theta\big(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Phi(x,\theta)\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi(x)+\sqrt{2}\Theta^{\alpha}\chi_{\alpha}(x)-\Theta\cdot\Theta F(x)\ .

Taking for example the
\Theta
-expansion of
\Xi
and
\mathcal{X}
we get:

\displaystyle\Xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Xi\big|+\sqrt{2}\Theta\cdot(\mathcal{D}\Xi)\big|-\frac{1}{4}\Theta\cdot\Theta(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\Xi)\big|=4F^{\dagger}+\frac{4}{3}M\phi^{\dagger}

(3.55)

\displaystyle+\Theta\cdot\Big\{-4i\sqrt{2}(\sigma^{\mu}\hat{D}_{\mu}\bar{\chi})+\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}b_{\mu}(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\chi})+\phi^{\dagger}\big(\frac{8}{3}(\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu})-\frac{4i}{3}(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\psi})M+\frac{4i}{3}\psi^{\mu}b_{\mu}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle-\Theta\cdot\Theta\Big\{-4e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}\hat{D}^{\mu}\phi^{\dagger}+\frac{8i}{3}b^{\mu}\hat{D}^{\mu}\bar{\chi}-\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}\bar{\chi}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{8}{3}M^{*}F^{\dagger}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2}i\bar{\chi}\cdot\bar{\psi}_{\mu}b^{\mu}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}\bar{\chi}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu\mu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}b_{\nu}+\phi^{\dagger}\Big(-\frac{2}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\big|-\frac{8}{9}MM^{*}+\frac{4}{9}b_{\mu}b^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{4i}{3}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{\mu}}b^{\mu}+\frac{2}{3}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\cdot\bar{\psi}^{\mu}M+\frac{2}{3}\psi_{\nu}\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}b^{\mu}+\frac{4i}{3}\bar{\psi}^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}+\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho})\Big\}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\big|+\sqrt{2}\Theta\cdot(\mathcal{D}\mathcal{X})\big|-\frac{1}{4}\Theta\cdot\Theta(\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{X})\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Xi+\Theta\cdot\Big\{-4\sqrt{2}g(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\chi})v_{\mu}+8ig\phi^{\dagger}\lambda+4ig\phi^{\dagger}v_{\nu}(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\psi_{\mu})\Big\}

\displaystyle-\Theta\cdot\Theta\Big\{8igv_{\mu}\hat{D}^{\mu}\phi^{\dagger}-4\sqrt{2}ig\bar{\chi}\cdot\bar{\lambda}+4ig\phi^{\dagger}\hat{D}_{\mu}v^{\mu}+\frac{8}{3}g\phi^{\dagger}v_{\mu}b^{\mu}-4gD\phi^{\dagger}+4g^{2}\phi^{\dagger}v_{\mu}v^{\mu}\Big\}\ .

We have seen in Subsection
3.3.1
that the variation of the auxiliary field
F
is not a total derivative. However, by defining the chiral density
\Delta
, which plays the rôle of an invariant measure in curved superspace, the action can be written in a fully covariant form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\int d^{4}x\,d^{2}\Theta\Delta f(\Phi)+\mathrm{h.c.}

(3.56)

with
f(\Phi)
a holomorphic function of chiral superfields. By imposing that the action transforms under a supergravity transformation
Eq. 3.36
as a total derivative, the chiral density must then transform as:

\displaystyle\delta\Delta=-\big(-\big)^{|\bar{M}|}\partial_{\bar{M}}\big(\eta^{\bar{M}}\Delta\big)

(3.57)

with
\eta^{\bar{M}}
the supergravity transformation parameters written with the new
\Theta-
variables:

\displaystyle\eta^{\bar{M}}(x,\Theta)=\eta^{\bar{M}}_{(0)}(x)+\Theta^{\alpha}\eta^{\bar{M}}_{(1)\alpha}(x)+\Theta\cdot\Theta\eta^{\bar{M}}_{(2)}(x)\ .

From the invariance of
Eq. 3.56
under supergravity transformations, the superfield
\Delta\Phi
must then transforms as a chiral density,
i.e.
,
\delta(\Delta\Phi)=-(-)^{\bar{M}}\partial_{\bar{M}}\left(\eta^{\bar{M}}\Delta\Phi\right)
which leads to the transformation of the chiral superfield
\Phi
:

\displaystyle\delta\Phi=-\eta^{\bar{M}}\partial_{\bar{M}}\Phi\ .

Developing this relation reads:

\displaystyle\delta\Phi=\big(-\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}_{(0)}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\phi-\sqrt{2}\eta^{\alpha}_{(0)}\chi_{\alpha}\big)+\Theta^{\alpha}\big(\sqrt{2}\eta_{\alpha(0)}F-\eta^{\mu}_{(0)}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\chi_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}_{(1)\alpha}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\phi-\eta^{\beta}_{(1)\alpha}\chi_{\beta}\big)

\displaystyle-\Theta\cdot\Theta\big(-\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}_{(0)}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}F+\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}_{(2)}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\phi-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}_{(1)}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tilde{\mu}}\chi_{\alpha}+\sqrt{2}\eta^{\alpha}_{(2)}\chi_{\alpha}-\eta^{\alpha}_{(1)\alpha}F\big)\ .

By comparing this result with (
3.46
,
3.47
,
3.48
), the form of
\eta^{\bar{M}}
can be found:

\displaystyle\eta^{\tilde{\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\Theta\sigma^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\epsilon}+\Theta\cdot\Theta\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\sigma}^{\tilde{\mu}}\sigma_{\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\epsilon}\ ,

\displaystyle\eta^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\alpha}-i\Theta\sigma^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\epsilon}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha}+\Theta\cdot\Theta\Big(\frac{1}{3}M^{*}\epsilon^{\alpha}-i\omega_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\alpha\beta}\big(\sigma^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\epsilon}\big)_{\beta}+\frac{1}{6}b_{\mu}\big(\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}\big)^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\alpha}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\sigma}^{\tilde{\nu}}\sigma^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\epsilon}\Big)\ .

Knowing the form of
\eta^{\bar{M}}(x,\Theta)
and using
Eq. 3.57
, the variation of the components of the chiral density can also be calculated.

The link with the vielbein can now be made. By selecting a chiral density
\mathcal{E}
such as its lowest component reduces to
\mathcal{E}\big|=\mathrm{det}\big(e_{\tilde{\mu}}{}^{\mu}\big)\equiv e
, we then introduce a relation between the chiral density and the vierbein. Step-by-step, it is possible to identify the components of
\mathcal{E}
, following
Eq. 3.57
and the transformation of the supergravity multiplet under supergravity transformations (
3.39
,
3.40
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\Big(1+i\Theta\sigma^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}-\Theta\cdot\Theta\big(M^{*}+\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\sigma}^{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\nu}}\big)\Big)\ .

(3.58)

3.4.2
Compact form of the supergravity action

We will now turn to the construction of the invariant action in supergravity. We suppose a compact real Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and the vector superfields
V=V^{a}T_{a}
associated to the adjoint representation of
\mathfrak{g}
. The chiral superfields
\Phi^{i}
are in a representation
\mathfrak{R}
of
\mathfrak{g}
whilst the anti-chiral superfields
\Phi^{\dagger}_{i^{*}}
are in the complex conjugate representation
\overline{\mathfrak{R}}
. The (anti-) chiral superfields contain the matter fields whereas the vector superfields
V
introduce the gauge interaction in the theory.

All the necessary tools have been introduced to construct a compact form of the
N=1
supergravity action in four dimensions. First of all, we recall the action of an
N=1
supersymmetry theory (derived in
[
6
]
[
7
]
). Three functions are needed to fully specify the dynamics of the model: the superpotential
W(\Phi)
a holomorphic function leading to the Yukawa interactions, the Kähler potential
K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})
, a real function defining the kinetic terms and the gauge kinetic function
h_{ab}
, a holomorphic function (
a
and
b
are indices in the adjoint representation of
G
). Recall that
N=1
supersymmetry action can be written as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{SUSY}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int d^{4}xd^{2}\theta\left(-\frac{1}{4}\bar{D}\cdot\bar{D}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})\right)

(3.59)

\displaystyle+\int d^{4}xd^{2}\theta W(\Phi)+\frac{1}{16g^{2}}\int d^{4}xd^{2}\theta h_{ab}\mathcal{W}^{\alpha a}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}^{b}+\mathrm{h.c.}\Bigg)

with
D_{\alpha}
the covariant derivative of supersymmetry.

For extending this action in the supergravity context we must:

1)

substitute the operator
D\cdot D
by
\mathcal{D}\cdot\mathcal{D}-8\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}
,

2)

change the Grassmann variable
\theta
to the new variables
\Theta
,

3)

add the chiral density
\mathcal{E}
to have invariance under supergravity transformations.

The
N=1
supergravity action in four dimensions can then be written as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8}\int d^{4}x\,d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}\Big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\Big)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}

(3.60)

\displaystyle+\int d^{4}x\,d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}W(\Phi)+\frac{1}{16g^{2}}\int d^{4}x\,d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}h_{ab}(\Phi)\mathcal{W}^{\alpha a}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}{}^{b}+\mathrm{h.c.}\ .

It can be observed that the term involving the Kähler potential is not obtained from the process mentioned above. This form is essential to get a correctly normalised action. Moreover, we see that reintroducing the Planck mass in the exponential and taking the low energy limit (
m_{p}\rightarrow\infty
):

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8}m_{p}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{3m_{p}^{2}}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}=\frac{3}{8}m_{p}^{2}-\frac{1}{8}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})+\mathcal{O}\left(1/m_{p}\right)

leads to the correct kinetic terms in
Eq. 3.59
with the pure gravity part of the action (given by
Eq. 3.65
).

3.4.3
Super-Weyl rescaling and Kähler transformation

In Subsection
3.2.3
, we have introduced Super-Weyl transformations and obtained the transformation laws of the fundamental objects of supergravity. We will see that Super-Weyl transformations coupled to Kähler transformations are symmetries of the supergravity action
Eq. 3.60
.

The transformation of the chiral density
\mathcal{E}
under super-Weyl transformations can be complex to compute. It is then more convenient to analyse the variation components-by-components,
i.e.
, identifying
\delta_{\Sigma}e
and
\delta_{\Sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}
with
\Sigma
the transformation parameter (a chiral superfield). Introducing the spinorial superfield:

\displaystyle S^{\alpha}=\Theta^{\alpha}\big(2\Sigma^{\dagger}-\Sigma\big)\big|+\Theta\cdot\Theta\mathcal{D}^{\alpha}\Sigma\big|

and regrouping the variations of the various fields in
\mathcal{E}
we can rewrite the transformation
\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
as:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\Sigma\mathcal{E}+\partial_{\Theta^{\alpha}}\big(S^{\alpha}\mathcal{E}\big)\ .

(3.61)

General Super-Weyl transformations acting on chiral and vector superfields are defined as:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Phi=w\Sigma\Phi\quad,\quad\delta_{\Sigma}V=w^{\prime}\big(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\big)V\ ,

where
w
and
w^{\prime}
are the Weyl weights of respectively the chiral and the vector superfield. In the
\Theta
-formalism, the variation of the chiral superfield modifies as:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w\Sigma\Phi-S^{\alpha}\partial_{\Theta^{\alpha}}\Phi\ .

By assuming a vanishing weight for the chiral superfield,
i.e.
,
\delta_{\Sigma}\Phi=-S^{\alpha}\partial_{\Theta^{\alpha}}\Phi
, as well as for the vector superfield, the superpotential
W(\Phi)
and the gauge kinetic function
h_{ab}
have also vanishing weight. Thus:

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\big(\mathcal{E}W\big)=6\Sigma\mathcal{E}W+\partial_{\Theta^{\alpha}}\big(S^{\alpha}\mathcal{E}W\big)\quad,\quad\delta_{\Sigma}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}=-3\Sigma\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}-S^{\beta}\partial_{\Theta^{\beta}}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Sigma}\Big(\mathcal{E}\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}\Big)=\mathcal{E}\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\left(2\left(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}\right)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\Theta^{\alpha}}\left(S^{\alpha}\mathcal{E}\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}\right)

which leads (considering now a finite transformation) to the following variation of the supergravity Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8}\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)e^{2\big(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger}\big)}e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}+\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}e^{6\Sigma}W(\Phi)

(3.62)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16g^{2}}\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}h(\Phi)_{ab}\mathcal{W}^{\alpha a}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}^{b}+\mathrm{h.c.}\ .

For further use, it is also important to give the transformations of the various fields of the theory under Weyl transformations:

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow e^{(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger})|}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\quad,\quad e

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{2(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger})|}e\quad,\quad\chi^{i}\rightarrow e^{(\Sigma-2\Sigma^{\dagger})|}\chi^{i}\ ,

(3.63)

\displaystyle\lambda_{a}\rightarrow e^{-3\Sigma|}\lambda_{a}\quad,\quad\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{(2\Sigma-\Sigma^{\dagger})|}\big(\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}-i\bar{\sigma}_{\tilde{\mu}}\mathcal{D}\Sigma|\big)\quad,\quad v_{\mu}^{a}\rightarrow e^{-(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger})|}v_{\mu}^{a}\ ,

\displaystyle D^{a}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{-2(\Sigma+\Sigma^{\dagger})|}D^{a}\ .

Note that this transformation induces also a gravitino-shift which will be important in the sequel.

Two points can be mentioned:

1)

the action is not invariant under Super-Weyl (superconformal) transformations,

2)

the gauge term is invariant under Super-Weyl (superconformal) transformations.

We now consider another type of transformation called Kähler transformation. This transformation involves a modification of the Kähler potential as

\displaystyle K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})\rightarrow K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})+F(\Phi)+F^{*}(\Phi^{\dagger})\ ,

where
F(\Phi)
is a gauge-singlet holomorphic function of the chiral superfields
\Phi
. Imposing that the superpotential transforms as:

\displaystyle W\rightarrow e^{-F}W\ ,

the covariant derivative of the superpotential
\mathcal{D}_{i}W=W_{i}+K_{i}W
transforms as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{i}W\rightarrow e^{-F}\mathcal{D}_{i}W\ .

Applying a Kähler transformation to the supergravity Lagrangian leads to:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8}\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K}e^{-\frac{1}{3}\big(F+F^{\dagger}\big)}+\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}e^{-F}W(\Phi)

(3.64)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16g^{2}}\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}h(\Phi)_{ab}\mathcal{W}^{\alpha a}\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}^{b}+\mathrm{h.c.}\ .

Comparing the transformations
Eq. 3.64
and
Eq. 3.62
, the Kähler transformation can then be seen as a superconformal transformation. Combining the two transformations with
F=6\Sigma
produce then a symmetry of the supergravity action.

3.5
Computation of the supergravity action

All the tools are now present for expanding in components the Lagrangian of supergravity (
Eq. 3.60
). The pure supergravity action will be given, and the gauge & matter coupled to supergravity will be expanded. The second part is simple but lengthy. The explicit calculation can be found in
[
6
]
[
7
]
.

We will also see that this expansion generates a Brans-Dicke normalisation
[
33
]
in the kinetic terms. A Super-Weyl rescaling must then be applied in order to recover correctly normalised couplings.

3.5.1
The pure supergravity action

The first term in
Eq. 3.60
contains the pure supergravity Lagrangian (when the Kähler potential vanish
K=0
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{Pure\,SUGRA}}=-3\int d^{2}\Theta\,\mathcal{E}\mathcal{R}+\mathrm{h.c.}\ .

(3.65)

Using the various derivatives of the chiral superfield
\mathcal{R}

Eq. 3.58
and the chiral density
\mathcal{E}

Eq. 3.58
, the computation is straightforward:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{Pure\,SUGRA}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}ee_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\frac{1}{4}e\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\Big(\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}-\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\Big)

(3.66)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}e\big(|M|^{2}+b_{\mu}b^{\mu}\big)\ .

The first term corresponds to the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian for the helicity-2 graviton whilst the second is the Rarita-Schwinger Lagrangian describing the spin-
3/2
gravitino. The two last terms involving
M
and
b_{\mu}
show that those fields does not propagate,
i.e.
, they are the two auxiliary fields of the supergravity multiplets.

3.5.2
Matter / Gauge coupling to Supergravity

To calculate the matter-gauge interactions couplings, we define the kinetic energy
\Omega
:

\Omega(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})=-3e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})}=W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})W^{I}(\Phi)\ ,

(3.67)

where
W^{I}
and
W_{I}
are (anti-)holomorphic functions depending on chiral (anti-chiral) superfields. Analogously as in supersymmetric theory (see
[
6
]
,
[
7
]
and
[
4
]
) we have:

\displaystyle W^{I}(\Phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W^{I}+\sqrt{2}\Theta\cdot\big(W^{I}_{i}\chi^{i}\big)-\Theta\cdot\Theta\big(W^{I}_{i}F^{i}+\frac{1}{2}W_{ij}^{I}\chi^{i}\cdot\chi^{j}\big)\ ,

(3.68)

\displaystyle W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W_{I}+\sqrt{2}\bar{\Theta}\cdot\big(W_{I}^{i*}\bar{\chi}_{i*}\big)-\bar{\Theta}\cdot\bar{\Theta}\big(W_{I}^{i*}F_{i*}^{\dagger}+\frac{1}{2}W^{i^{*}j^{*}}_{I}{}{\bar{\chi}_{i*}\cdot\bar{\chi}_{j*}}\big)

(3.69)

(we introduce now
W^{I}\equiv W^{I}(\phi)
and similarly for
W_{I}
). The development in components of the various terms is relatively easy but time-consuming. We will only give the main steps of the calculation.

Considering the Kähler part of the supergravity Lagrangian, using the definition of
\Omega(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})

Eq. 3.67
, it follows:

\mathcal{L}_{K}=\int d^{2}\Theta\,\frac{3}{8}\mathcal{E}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)e^{-\frac{1}{3}K(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})}=-\int d^{2}\Theta\,\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{E}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)\Omega(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})\ .

(3.70)

We first observe that since
\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}
acts as a projector from anti-chiral to chiral superfields (see Subsection
3.3.1
):

\displaystyle\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)\Omega(\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\big)W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})\Big)W^{I}(\Phi)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{I}W^{I}(\Phi)\ .

Using the Wess-Zumino gauge
Eq. 3.49
(with
V^{3}=0
), the expression of
\mathcal{X}_{I}
can be decomposed in three terms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger}e^{-2gV})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\mathcal{D}}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{D}}-8\mathcal{R}\right)\Big(W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})+\frac{\partial W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})}{\partial\Phi^{\dagger}_{i^{*}}}\left(-2g(\Phi^{\dagger}V)_{i^{*}}+2g^{2}(\Phi^{\dagger}V^{2})_{i^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle+2g^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})}{\partial\Phi^{\dagger}_{i^{*}}\partial\Phi^{\dagger}_{j^{*}}}(\Phi^{\dagger}V)_{i^{*}}(\Phi^{\dagger}V)_{j^{*}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Xi_{I}+\mathcal{X}_{I}^{(g)}+\mathcal{X}_{I}^{(g^{2})}\ ,

with
\Xi_{I}
defined as in
Eq. 3.55
by substituting the components of the superfield
\Phi^{\dagger}
by the components of
W_{I}(\Phi^{\dagger})
. The product of this term with the chiral density
\mathcal{E}

Eq. 3.58
leads then to
\mathcal{L}_{K}
.

The computation of the superpotential and the gauge parts of the supergravity Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{W+gauge}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}W(\Phi)+\frac{1}{16g^{2}}\int d^{2}\Theta\mathcal{E}h_{ab}(\Phi)\mathcal{W}^{\alpha a}\mathcal{W}^{b}_{\alpha}+\text{h.c.}

is easier after decomposing the gauge kinetic function
h_{ab}(\Phi)
in the same manner as
W_{I}(\Phi)

Eq. 3.68
and using
\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
in
Eq. 3.53
.

The final Lagrangian takes the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{kin}+\mathcal{L}_{int}+\mathcal{L}_{aux}

with
\mathcal{L}_{kin}
containing the kinetic terms,
\mathcal{L}_{int}
the interaction terms and
\mathcal{L}_{aux}
the terms evolving the auxiliary fields
(F^{i},D^{a},M,b_{\mu})
. Note that this last term can be eliminated through the Euler-Lagrange relations leading to new couplings:

\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial N}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial N^{*}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial F^{i}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial F^{\dagger}_{i^{*}}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial b_{\mu}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{aux}}{\partial D^{a}}=0

with
N=M+3(\mathrm{ln}\Omega)^{i^{*}}F^{\dagger}_{i^{*}}
.

However, it turns out that after eliminated the auxiliary fields, the various kinetic terms are not correctly normalised. For instance, we obtain for gravity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{K}=-\frac{1}{6}e\Omega e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\big|

(3.71)

which corresponds to a non-proper normalisation (called Brans-Dicke normalisation). To obtain a correctly normalised action, we can perform a Weyl rescaling (see
Eq. 3.63
):

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow e^{-\lambda}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}\quad\quad(e\rightarrow e^{4\lambda}e)\ .

(3.72)

Choosing
e^{2\lambda}=-\frac{3}{\Omega}
, we get for the gravity kinetic term:

-\frac{1}{6}e\Omega e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|\rightarrow-\frac{1}{6}e^{2\lambda}e\Omega e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\cdots=\frac{1}{2}ee_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\cdots

where the dots indicate other terms coming from the transformation of the spin-connection
\omega_{\tilde{\mu}MN}
. Similarly, the fermions kinetic terms read after the expansion,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{kin.\ fermions}=-\frac{1}{12}e\Omega\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}-\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\right)+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}e\left(\Omega_{i}\chi^{i}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{\mu\nu}+\Omega^{i^{*}}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}e\Omega^{i^{*}}{}_{i}\left(\chi^{i}\sigma^{\mu}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}-\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\chi^{i}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\right)\ ,

After the Weyl rescaling

\displaystyle\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow e^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda}\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\quad,\quad\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow e^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda}\bar{\psi}_{\tilde{\mu}}\quad,\quad\chi^{i}\rightarrow e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda}\chi^{i}\quad,\quad\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\rightarrow e^{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\ ,

the fermions kinetic part are correctly normalised, but the gravitino-fermion mixing term remains present. This rescaling must then be followed by the gravitino-shift:

\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow\psi_{\tilde{\mu}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}i}{2}\Omega^{-1}\Omega^{i^{*}}\sigma_{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\ ,

(3.73)

which suppress this mixing term. Those two transformations can be embedded in a single Super-Weyl transformations
Eq. 3.63
with the transformation parameters
3
3

3

The transformation can be fully embedded in a single Super-Weyl transformation since the Weyl rescaling and the gravitino-shift commute.
:

\displaystyle\Sigma=\frac{1}{2}\lambda+\sqrt{2}\Theta\cdot\lambda_{i}\chi^{i}-\Theta\cdot\Theta\mathcal{F}\quad,\quad\Sigma^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{2}\lambda+\sqrt{2}\bar{\Theta}\cdot\bar{\lambda}^{i^{*}}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}-\bar{\Theta}\cdot\bar{\Theta}\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}\ .

(3.74)

This transformation is the key point of the complexity of the calculus.

Applying the Weyl transformations associated with the gravitino-shift to the supergravity action
Eq. 3.60
allow to obtain a correctly normalised action. This is a very tedious computation, mostly due to the presence of the gravitino-shift
Eq. 3.73
.

The properly normalised Lagrangian of the
N=1
supergravity in four dimensions follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{SUGRA}=\mathcal{L}_{kin.}+\mathcal{L}_{int.}\ .

(3.75)

The kinetic part reads:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{kin.}=\frac{1}{2}e_{\mu}{}^{\tilde{\mu}}e_{\nu}{}^{\tilde{\nu}}R_{\tilde{\mu}\tilde{\nu}}{}^{\mu\nu}\Big|+\frac{1}{4}e\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\big[\psi_{\mu}\sigma_{\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\nu\rho}-\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\sigma}\psi_{\nu\rho}\big]-\frac{1}{4}eh_{ab}{}^{R}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu b}+\frac{1}{4}eh_{ab}{}^{I}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}{}^{*}F^{\mu\nu b}

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}eh_{ab}^{R}\big(\lambda^{a}\sigma^{\mu}\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{b}-\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\lambda^{a}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{b}\big)+\frac{1}{2}eh_{ab}^{I}\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\left(\lambda^{a}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{b}\right)-\frac{i}{2}eK^{i^{*}}{}_{i}\Big[\chi^{i}\sigma^{\mu}\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}-\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\chi^{i}\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\chi}_{i^{*}}\Big]
