\{\phi^{a}\}
coming from the non-canonical of the Kähler potential in the
\phi^{a}
-sector.
We can now compute the scalar potential (with a perturbative expansion of the exponential):

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\hat{K}}\left(1+\frac{\tilde{K}}{m_{p}^{2}}\right)\Bigg[\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}\mathcal{D}_{i}W(K^{-1})^{i}{}_{j}\mathcal{D}^{j}\overline{W}+\mathcal{D}_{p}W\mathcal{D}^{p}\overline{W}+\mathcal{D}_{a}W(K^{-1})^{a}{}_{a*}\mathcal{D}^{a^{\ast}}\overline{W}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{p}}\Big(\mathcal{D}_{i}W(K^{-1})^{i}{}_{a^{\ast}}\mathcal{D}^{a^{\ast}}\overline{W}+\mathrm{h.c.}\Big)-\frac{3}{m_{p}^{2}}\left|W\right|^{2}\Bigg]\ .

After a lengthy computation, the scalar potential is:

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{p}^{2}\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\big|\xi_{3/2}\big|^{2}}-3\big)+e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{p}\big|{\cal I}_{p}+M_{4}^{3}\partial_{p}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}+M_{4}^{4}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\partial^{a^{\ast}}\bar{\omega}_{0}\langle(\Lambda^{-1})^{a}{}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle\Big)

(4.22)

\displaystyle+M_{4}^{2}\Big((\langle\phi^{a}\rangle+\phi^{a})(\langle\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle+\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}})\Big)\Big(\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}{\cal S}^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\big[\bar{m}_{3/2}S^{p}\;({\cal S}_{p})^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}+{\mbox{h}.c.}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{4}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{p}S^{\dagger}_{q}\;({\cal S}^{q}{}_{p})^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big((\langle S^{p}\rangle+S^{p})(\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle+S^{\dagger}_{p})\Big)\Big(\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}T+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\big[\bar{m}_{3/2}S^{r}T_{r}+\mbox{h.c.}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{4}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{r}S^{\dagger}_{t}T^{t}{}_{s}\Big)+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{p}S^{\dagger}_{q}\Big(\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{q}-3{\cal I}_{p}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Bigg\{\Big(M_{4}^{2}(\langle\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle+\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}})(\langle\phi^{a}\rangle+\phi^{a})\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle+(\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle+S^{\dagger}_{p})(\langle S^{p}\rangle+S^{p})\Big)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 65.44142pt(\langle S^{q}\rangle+S^{q}){\cal I}_{q}\Big(\bar{m}_{3/2}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{\dagger}_{r}\bar{\cal I}^{r}\Big)+\mbox{h.c.}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(M_{4}^{2}(\langle\phi^{a}\rangle+\phi^{a})(\langle\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle+\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}})\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle+(\langle S^{p}\rangle+S^{p})(\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle+S^{\dagger}_{p})\Big)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt\Big(\sum\limits_{r}\big|{\cal I}_{r}\big|^{2}+M_{4}^{3}{\cal I}^{r}\partial_{r}\omega_{0}+M_{4}^{3}\bar{{\cal I}}_{r}\partial^{r}\bar{\omega}^{0}\Big)

\displaystyle+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Bigg\{\bar{m}_{3/2}M_{4}^{3}R^{b}{}_{a}(\langle\phi^{a}\rangle+\phi^{a})\partial_{b}\omega_{0}+\frac{M_{4}^{3}}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}(R^{p})^{b}{}_{a}(\langle\phi^{a}\rangle+\phi^{a})S^{\dagger}_{p}\partial_{b}\omega_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\big[\bar{m}_{3/2}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{\dagger}_{q}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\big](\langle S^{p}\rangle+S^{p})\big[{\cal I}_{p}+M_{4}^{3}\partial_{p}\omega_{0}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\frac{M_{4}^{3}}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}(\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle+S^{\dagger}_{p})\bar{\cal I}^{p}\Delta\omega_{0}+\mbox{h.c.}\Bigg\}+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\bar{m}_{3/2}S^{p}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}-3\ {\cal I}_{p}+\mbox{h.c.}\big)

\displaystyle+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}M_{4}^{3}\Big(\Delta\mathfrak{d}_{z}\omega_{0}\big[\frac{\bar{m}_{3/2}}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{\dagger}_{q}\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\big]-3\Delta\omega_{0}\big[\bar{m}_{3/2}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{\dagger}_{q}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\big]+\mbox{h.c.}\Big)

where we recall that
m^{\prime}_{3/2}=m_{3/2}/\xi_{3/2}
and we define

\displaystyle{\cal S}^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\big|\xi_{3/2}\big|^{2}}\Big(\langle\partial^{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{b}(\Lambda^{-1})^{b}{}_{b^{\ast}}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{b^{\ast}}{}_{a}-\partial^{z}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle\Big)+\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle\big(\frac{1}{\big|\xi_{3/2}\big|^{2}}-2\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Lambda^{a^{*}}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{\big|\xi_{3/2}\big|^{2}}\ \tilde{\cal S}^{a^{*}}{}_{a}\ ,

\displaystyle({\cal S}_{p})^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}\Big(\langle\partial^{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{b}(\Lambda^{-1})^{b}{}_{b^{\ast}}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{b^{\ast}}{}_{a}-\partial^{z}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle\Big)\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}+\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}-2{\cal I}_{p}\Big)\ ,

\displaystyle({\cal S}^{q}{}_{p})^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\langle\partial^{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{b}(\Lambda^{-1})^{b}{}_{b^{\ast}}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{b^{\ast}}{}_{a}-\partial^{z}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle\Big)\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{q}+\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle(\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{q}-2{\cal I}_{p}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\big)\ ,

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\big|\xi_{3/2}\big|^{2}}-2\ ,

\displaystyle T_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}-2{\cal I}_{p}\ ,

\displaystyle T^{p}{}_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{q}\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{q}-2{\cal I}_{q}\bar{\cal I}^{q}\ ,

\displaystyle R^{a}{}_{b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{a}_{b}-\frac{1}{\bar{\xi}_{3/2}}\langle(\Lambda^{-1})^{a}{}_{b^{\ast}}\partial_{z}\Lambda^{b^{\ast}}{}_{b}\rangle\ ,

\displaystyle(R^{p})^{a}{}_{b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\cal I}^{p}\delta^{a}{}_{b}-\mathfrak{d}^{z}\bar{\cal I}^{p}\langle(\Lambda^{-1})^{a}{}_{b^{\ast}}\partial^{z}\Lambda^{b^{\ast}}{}_{b}\rangle\ .

At this stage, we can identify several types of contributions:

•

a non-vanishing cosmological constant
\Lambda=\langle V\rangle
:

\displaystyle\big\langle V\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{p}^{2}\big(\big|m^{\prime}_{3/2}\big|^{2}-3\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}\big)+e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{p}\big|{\cal I}_{p}+M_{4}^{3}\langle\partial_{p}\omega_{0}\rangle\big|^{2}+M_{4}^{4}\langle\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\partial^{a^{\ast}}\bar{\omega}_{0}(\Lambda^{-1})^{a}{}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle\Big)

(4.23)

\displaystyle+\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}M_{4}^{2}\langle\phi^{a}\rangle\langle\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle{\cal S}^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}+\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}\langle S^{p}\rangle\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle T

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(M_{4}^{2}\langle\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle\langle\phi^{a}\rangle\langle\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\rangle+\langle S^{\dagger}_{p}\rangle\langle S^{p}\rangle\Big)\Bigg\{\Big(\bar{m}_{3/2}\langle S^{q}\rangle{\cal I}_{q}+\mbox{h.c.}\Big)

\displaystyle+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{r}\big|{\cal I}_{r}\big|^{2}+M_{4}^{3}{\cal I}^{r}\langle\partial_{r}\omega_{0}\rangle+M_{4}^{3}\bar{{\cal I}}_{r}\langle\partial^{r}\bar{\omega}^{0}\rangle\Big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\bar{m}_{3/2}M_{4}^{3}R^{b}{}_{a}\langle\phi^{a}\rangle\langle\partial_{b}\omega_{0}\rangle+\bar{m}_{3/2}\langle S^{p}\rangle\big[{\cal I}_{p}+M_{4}^{3}\langle\partial_{p}\omega_{0}\rangle\big]+\mbox{h.c.}\Big)\ .

We assume:

\displaystyle\langle V\rangle=0

which may lead to a fine-tuning of the parameters and is usual in supergravity.

•

the classical SUSY potential (see
Eq. 3.80
):

\displaystyle e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{p}\big|{\cal I}_{p}+M_{4}^{3}\partial_{p}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}+M_{4}^{4}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\partial^{a^{\ast}}\bar{\omega}_{0}\langle(\Lambda^{-1})^{a}{}_{a^{\ast}}\rangle\Big)\ .

•

soft-breaking terms associated with the
\{\phi^{a}\}
and
\{S^{p}\}
sectors. For example, from the second and fourth lines of
4.22
, we can identify contributions to the soft-breaking mass terms

\displaystyle|m_{3/2}|^{2}\Big(M_{4}^{2}\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}\mathcal{S}^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}\phi^{a}+|S^{p}|^{2}T\Big)\ .

•

the new hard-breaking terms. For instance, we found in the third and fifth lines the couplings
4.12

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{p}^{4}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}S^{r}S^{\dagger}_{t}\Big(M_{4}^{2}\phi^{a}\phi^{\dagger}_{a^{\ast}}(\mathcal{S}^{t}{}_{r})^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}+|S^{p}|^{2}T^{t}{}_{r}\Big)

(4.24)

which contribute to the last term of
4.12
.

Based on the scalar potential
Eq. 4.22
, the minimisation equations of the potential can be obtained as well as the element of the mass matrix. They can be found in Appendix
Appendix D
.

4.2.3
Differences between S2MSSM and N2MSSM

The structure of the Non-Soni-Weldon solutions imposes the presence of at least two hybrid fields
S^{p}
(
p=1,2
) to generate couplings between the
\mathcal{U}
field and the matter sector
\{\tilde{\Phi}^{a}\}
. Since those new fields must be singlet under the gauge group of the matter sector, the field content is equivalent to a two-singlets extension of the MSSM, the N2MSSM (see Section
3.7.2
). The N2MSSM is, however, defined in the context of Soni-Weldon solutions, resulting so to only soft-breaking terms in the potential.

Nonetheless, the presence/absence of hard SUSY breaking-terms is not the only difference between those two models. For instance, the two couplings
\lambda_{1}S^{1}H_{U}\cdot H_{D}
and
\lambda_{2}S^{2}H_{U}\cdot H_{D}
in the N2MSSM are completely independent whilst the equivalent couplings in the S2MSSM are correlated from the structure of the superpotential and the form of the
\mathcal{U}
-field.

Of course, the hard-breaking terms will contribute to all the phenomenological analysis of the NSW solutions. The electroweak symmetry breaking conditions will be modified by the presence of such new breaking terms. In addition, new contributions on the mass matrices could also affect the mass spectrum of the model. Indeed, the presence of
\phi\phi^{\dagger}SS^{\dagger}
term in the potential will contribute at tree-level and loop-level through diagrams in
Figure 4.1
(proportional to the singlet mass after renormalisation). The effects of the new S-sector on the Higgs sector is investigated in
section 4.4
. Moreover, those couplings could also push the sfermion sector to a higher energy scale, leading potentially to Split-SUSY-like models
[
57
]
[
58
]
. We recall that Split-SUSY models are based on the paradigm that the naturalness problem is ignored, assuming the anthropic principle. The supersymmetry breaking scale is assumed to be very high (for example, at the GUT scale
M_{SUSY}\approx M_{GUT}\approx 10^{15}\ \mathrm{GeV}
). As a consequence, the masses of the fermionic superpartners of the standard model are in the order of
M_{GUT}
, which leads to interesting phenomenological properties (such as long-lived gluino) and to highly fine-tuned models. The differences between Split-SUSY models and NSW solutions is essentially due to the presence of hard-breaking terms. Nonetheless, a heavy sfermionic sector coupled with a heavy Higgs state can be a consequence of a really heavy singlet state pushing all the mass spectrum to higher energy (from the loop effect
Figure 4.1
). Following a see-saw effect, a light singlet state can then appear in the spectrum. Finally, as mentioned in
subsection 4.1.2
, the NSW solutions provide new effects which will modify the structure of the RGEs.

Figure 4.1
:
One-loop contribution on the mass of the field
\phi
.

4.3
W_{0}
not containing
S^{p}
fields: constraints and mass matrix

The potential
Eq. 4.22
leads to several new contributions coming from loop-effects involving
S
fields. While the terms in the form
(Q_{a})^{q}{}_{p}\phi^{a}\phi^{\dagger}_{a}S^{p}S^{\dagger}_{q}
and
{Q^{\prime}}^{r}{}_{t}S^{p}S^{\dagger}_{p}S^{t}S^{\dagger}_{r}
act on the diagonal elements of the mass matrix, the terms coming from the superpotential such as
D^{q}\mathcal{U}H_{U}\cdot H_{D}S^{\dagger}_{q}
contribute to the off-diagonal elements and potentially generate push-up effects. Those mechanisms could end up with a reduction of the fine-tuning on the Higgs boson mass.

In this section, we present a numerical analysis of the effects of the
S
fields on the
\{\phi^{a}\}
sector (
a=1,\dots,m
). Considering several constraints such as the vanishing of the cosmological constant and the minimisation conditions of the potential, we investigate the order of magnitude of the
S
-loop in a simplified model:

•

the
v.e.vs
of the hybrid and matter sector are assumed to vanish
\langle S\rangle=\langle\phi\rangle=0
;

•

we also assume a canonical Kähler potential,
\Lambda^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}(z,z^{\dagger})=\delta^{a^{\ast}}{}_{a}
.

Following those approximations, the
S
-contribution to the gravitino mass vanish:

\displaystyle m_{3/2}=\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{K/(2m_{p}^{2})}\langle W\rangle=e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}/2}\frac{M_{4}^{3}}{m_{p}^{2}}\langle w\rangle

(see
subsection 4.2.1
). A gravitino mass
m_{3/2}
lower than
M_{1}
and
M_{4}
can then be obtained by assuming:

\displaystyle m_{p}M_{1}^{2}\approx M_{4}^{3}\ll m_{p}^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad|\langle w\rangle|\ll 1

(4.25)

with
|\langle\omega_{0}\rangle|\sim|\langle\omega_{1}\rangle|\sim\mathcal{O}(1)
, and
\omega_{0}
and
\omega_{1}
have opposite sign phases. This parametrisation may include a little fine-tuning that will not be considered for this first study.

4.3.1
Corrections on the Higgs sector

The mass matrix of the Higgs/
S
sector is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{H/S}^{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\mathcal{S}&(\mathcal{SH})^{t}\\
\hline\cr\mathcal{SH}&\mathcal{H}\end{array}\right)

with
\mathcal{S}
the submatrix in the hybrid sector,
\mathcal{H}
the submatrix in the Higgs sector and
\mathcal{SH}
the mixing elements. This matrix is quite similar to a Soni-Weldon solution of
Gravity-Mediated Supersymmetry Breaking
with singlets fields (such as NMSSM or N2MSSM). The presence of the hybrid sector can then already modify the Higgs spectrum at tree-level in the same manner as singlets from the N2MSSM do. In a desirable case, they can contribute to the Higgs boson mass of the Standard Model and possibly reduce a potential fine-tuning on the parameters. The two main methods (described in
5.1.2
for the NMSSM) to push the Higgs boson mass at tree-level are

•

contributing to the diagonal elements;

•

generating a push-up effect
via
a mixing term in the
\mathcal{SH}
submatrix.

In the context of the S2MSSM, the hard-breaking terms contribute at tree-level but at one-loop level too. We can identify in the full potential
Eq. 4.22
new supersymmetric breaking terms which contribute at one-loop level to each submatrix in
subsection 4.3.1
. The hard-breaking terms in
4.24
contribute to the diagonal elements of
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{H}
as (using the assumptions mentioned in this section):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{p}^{4}}e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\big(M_{4}^{2}\phi^{a}\phi^{\dagger}_{a}+S^{p}S^{\dagger}_{p}\big)\big(4|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}-2\big){\cal I}_{r}\bar{\cal I}^{t}S^{r}S^{\dagger}_{t}

(4.28)

where the two couplings involving
\Delta\omega_{0}
(in the two last lines of
4.22
) generate correction in the mixing part
\mathcal{SH}
through:

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\bar{\cal I}^{q}S^{\dagger}_{q}\mathcal{U}H_{U}\cdot H_{D}

(4.29)

by closing the
S
-loop. The negative sign can be problematic since it can lower the tree-level contribution of the Higgs boson mass. However, the sign can be reabsorbed in the definition of the
\mathcal{U}
-field and so resolve this issue. We will study the order of magnitude of those corrections.

4.3.2
Cosmological constant and minimisation of the potential

In this section, we present the calculation of the minimum of the potential, the minimisation equations and the mass matrix in the general case of
n
hybrid singlet fields
\{S^{p}\}
(
p=1,\dots,n
). We assume no direct couplings between the hybrid and the matter sector for a first approach,
i.e.

\partial_{p}\omega_{0}=0
. Note that this simplification naturally emerges in the case of one singlet
S
. Indeed, since the coupling between the matter and the hybrid sector is obtain
via
the linear combination of two
S
-fields, there is no direct coupling between
\phi
and
S
in the superpotential
W_{0}
. In this configuration, the minimum of the potential of
Eq. 4.23
can be written as:

\displaystyle{\left\langle V\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{p}|{\cal I}_{p}|^{2}+M_{4}^{4}\sum\limits_{a}\Big|\langle\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle\Big|^{2}\Big)+m_{p}^{2}(|m^{\prime}_{3/2}|^{2}-3|m_{3/2}|^{2})

(4.30)

where supersymmetry breaking is signaled by
\langle F^{I}F^{\dagger}_{I}\rangle\neq 0
with

\displaystyle\langle F^{I}F^{\dagger}_{I}\rangle=e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\sum\limits_{p}|{\cal I}_{p}|^{2}+M_{4}^{4}\sum\limits_{a}\Big|\langle\partial_{a}\;\omega_{0}\rangle\Big|^{2}\Big)+m_{p}^{2}\,|m_{3/2}^{\prime}|^{2}\ .

(4.31)

For simplification, we introduce the notation
|{\cal I}|^{2}=\sum\limits_{p}|{\cal I}_{p}|^{2}
. Assuming the vanishing of the cosmological constant allows us to express the parameter
|{\cal I}|^{2}
:

\displaystyle|{\cal I}|^{2}=e^{-\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}m_{p}^{2}(3|m_{3/2}|^{2}-|m^{\prime}_{3/2}|^{2})-M_{4}^{4}\sum\limits_{a}\Big|\langle\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle\Big|^{2}\ .

(4.32)

Through this relation, it is possible to suppress the
|{\cal I}|^{2}
-dependency in the mass matrix. Note also that due to the negative sign on the last term, and since
|{\cal I}|^{2}
must be positive, we have

\displaystyle|m^{\prime}_{3/2}|<\sqrt{3}|m_{3/2}|\Rightarrow|\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}w\rangle|<\sqrt{3}|\langle w\rangle|\ll 1\ .

(4.33)

Since
|{\cal I}|^{2}
controls the loop contributions (see
Eq. 4.28
and
Eq. 4.29
), a first hint to obtain non-negligible corrections is to suppress the negative terms in
Eq. 4.32
. Since the partial derivative
\partial_{a}\omega_{0}
will be eliminated using minimisation equations, a possible solution is to assume low value of
m^{\prime}_{3/2}
(
i.e.
, high values of
\xi_{3/2}
). Note however that taking too high value of
\xi_{3/2}
may correspond to a fine-tuning. The first derivatives of the potential can also be easily obtained:

\displaystyle\Big<\partial_{z}V\Big>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}\bar{\cal I}^{p}+M_{4}^{4}\langle\partial_{a}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\;\omega_{0}\rangle\langle\partial^{a}\;\bar{\omega}_{0}\rangle\Big)

(4.34)

\displaystyle+m_{p}^{2}\Big(m^{\prime\prime}_{3/2}\bar{m}^{\prime}_{3/2}-2m^{\prime}_{3/2}\bar{m}_{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\Big<\partial_{p}V\Big>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Big(\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}\bar{m}^{\prime}_{3/2}-2{\cal I}_{p}\bar{m}_{3/2}\Big)\ ,\quad\forall p=1,\ \dots,\ n

(4.35)

\displaystyle\Big<\partial_{a}V\big>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}M_{4}^{4}\langle\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\omega_{0}\rangle\langle\partial^{b}\bar{\omega}_{0}\rangle

(4.36)

\displaystyle+e^{\frac{1}{2}\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}M_{4}^{3}\Big(\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle\bar{m}^{\prime}_{3/2}-2\langle\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle\bar{m}_{3/2}\Big)\ ,\quad\forall a=1,\ \dots,\ m

The derivative along the
S
field
4.35
leads to a relation between
{\cal I}_{p}
and the covariant derivative
\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}
:

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{p}V\rangle=0\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\mathfrak{d}_{z}{\cal I}_{p}=2\bar{\xi}_{3/2}{\cal I}_{p}\quad\forall p=1,\dots,\ n\ .

(4.37)

Assuming one field
\phi
in the matter sector for simplicity, the two others constraints (
4.34
and
4.35
) can be rewritten in the following form:

\displaystyle M_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Re\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\partial^{\phi}\bar{\omega}_{0}\rangle}{Re\langle 2\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\bar{w}-\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\bar{w}\rangle}m_{p}^{2}

(4.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Im\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\partial^{\phi}\bar{\omega}_{0}\rangle}{Im\langle 2\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\bar{w}-\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\bar{w}\rangle}m_{p}^{2}\ ,\quad\forall a=1,\ \dots,\ m

for the
\langle\partial_{a}V\rangle
derivative and

\displaystyle M_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Re\langle\partial^{\phi}\bar{\omega}_{0}\big(2\bar{w}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}-\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\bar{w}\big)\rangle}{Re\left\langle 6|w|^{2}\overline{w}-4|\mathfrak{d}_{z}w|^{2}\overline{w}+(\overline{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}\overline{w})^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{z}w\right\rangle}m_{p}^{2}

(4.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Im\langle\partial^{\phi}\bar{\omega}_{0}\big(2\bar{w}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}-\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\bar{w}\big)\rangle}{Im\left\langle 6|w|^{2}\overline{w}-4|\mathfrak{d}_{z}w|^{2}\overline{w}+(\overline{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}\overline{w})^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{z}w\right\rangle}m_{p}^{2}

for
\langle\partial_{z}V\rangle
.

4.3.3
Mass matrix

To pursue our analysis, we first check the mass matrix structure. The mass matrix is calculated in the
\{\phi,S,z\}
basis. Denoting
\mathcal{M}_{1}
the real
m\times m
symmetric submatrix in the basis
\{\phi^{a}\}
(with their respecting eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m}
) and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
the real
(n+1)\times(n+1)
symmetric submatrix in the basis
\{S^{p},z\}
(with their eigenvalues
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n+1)}
), the mass matrix
\mathcal{M}
has then the following structure:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\begin{pmatrix}\phantom{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{1}&\varepsilon P^{t}\\
\varepsilon P&\varepsilon\mathcal{M}_{2}\end{pmatrix}

with
\varepsilon\sim 0
and
P
a real
(n+1)\times m
matrix. It can be shown that the eigenvalues of the matrix
\mathcal{M}
are at the first order of
\varepsilon
:
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m}
and
\varepsilon\mu_{1},\dots,\varepsilon\mu_{n+1}
, meaning that the matrix
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
can be diagonalised separately. The complete proof of this statement can be found in Appendix
E
. In order to estimate the order of magnitude of the S-loop, we consider the submatrix
\{S^{p},z\}
.

Implementing the constraints
4.37
in the general mass matrix (see Appendix
D
) in the case of the simplified model:

\displaystyle{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial S_{q}^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{p}{}^{q}|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}|m^{\prime}_{3/2}|^{2}+\frac{e^{|\left\langle z\right\rangle|^{2}}}{m_{p}^{2}}\left(4|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}-1\right)\mathcal{I}_{p}\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{q},

\displaystyle m_{p}^{-1}{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial z^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{|\left\langle z\right\rangle|^{2}/2}}{m_{p}}\left(\overline{m}^{\prime}_{3/2}-2\overline{\xi}_{3/2}\overline{m}^{\prime\prime}_{3/2}\right)\mathcal{I}_{p}

(4.40)

\displaystyle m_{p}^{-2}{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial z\partial z^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}e^{\big|\langle z\rangle\big|^{2}}\Bigg[\big|{\cal I}\big|^{2}\Big(1+4|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}\Big)+M_{4}^{4}\sum\limits_{a}\Big(\Big|\langle\partial_{a}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\omega_{0}\rangle\Big|^{2}+\Big|\langle\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle\Big|^{2}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\big|m^{\prime\prime}_{3/2}\big|^{2}-2\big|m_{3/2}\big|^{2}

Note that we do not compute the elements
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial z}\right\rangle}
and
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial z^{2}}\right\rangle}
since they can be fine-tuned to zero using the parameters
m^{\prime\prime\prime}_{3/2}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}{\cal I}_{p}
and so simplify the mass matrix structure. The others elements
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\phi^{a}\partial\phi^{b}}\right\rangle}
,
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial z\partial\phi^{a}}\right\rangle}
and
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial\phi^{a}}\right\rangle}
are not relevant due to the screening property of the mass matrix and
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial S^{q}}\right\rangle}=0
in the special case
\partial_{p}\omega_{0}=0
. Furthermore, all the elements in the form
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial X^{I}\partial X^{J}}\right\rangle}
and
{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial X^{{\dagger}}_{I}\partial X^{{\dagger}}_{J}}\right\rangle}
automatically vanish if the hidden sector is invariant under a
U(1)
symmetry.

If we want a high effect from the S-loop, the off-diagonal term must be important to boost the mixing between the
z
and
S^{p}
fields. Since
\bar{m}^{\prime}_{3/2}
is already constrained by the cosmological constant, we can assume:

\displaystyle|\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle|\sim\mathcal{O}(1)\ .

(4.41)

Due to this structure, the mass matrix has some interesting properties which we now demonstrate. We first rewrite the mass matrix in a more general form by highlighting the
{\cal I}_{p}
dependencies:

\displaystyle{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial S_{p}^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}=a+b|\mathcal{I}_{p}|^{2},\quad{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial S_{q}^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}=b\mathcal{I}_{p}\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\ ,

(4.42)

\displaystyle m_{p}^{-1}{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{p}\partial z^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}=c\mathcal{I}_{p}\ ,\quad m_{p}^{-2}{\left\langle\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial z\partial z^{\dagger}}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d\ .

(4.43)

The
(n+1)\times(n+1)
squared mass matrix can then be written in the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{2}\equiv a\mathbb{I}+\mathbb{A}\ ,\quad\text{with}\quad\mathbb{A}=\begin{pmatrix}[B]&[C]\\
[C^{\dagger}]&[D]\end{pmatrix},

(4.44)

where
\mathbb{A}
is a block matrix and we introduce
[B],[C],[D]
,
n\times n
,
1\times n
and
1\times 1
matrices respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left[B\right]_{p}{}^{q}&=b\mathcal{I}_{p}\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\ ,\\
\left[C\right]_{p}&=c\mathcal{I}_{p},\ [C^{\dagger}]^{q}=c^{*}\overline{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\left[D\right]_{1}\!\!{}^{1}&=d-a\ .\end{aligned}

(4.45)

The squared mass matrix
\mathbb{A}^{2}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[B].[B]+[C].[C^{\dagger}]&[B].[C]+[D][C]\\
[C^{\dagger}].[B]+[C^{\dagger}][D]&[C^{\dagger}].[C]+[D]^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}(b|\mathcal{I}|^{2}+\frac{|c|^{2}}{b})[B]&(b|\mathcal{I}|^{2}+d-a)[C]\\
(b|\mathcal{I}|^{2}+d-a)[C^{\dagger}]&(\frac{|c|^{2}}{d-a}+d-a)[D]\end{pmatrix}\ ,

showing that
\mathbb{A}
has the same block matrix structure of
\mathbb{A}
up to multiplicative factors that are function of
|\mathcal{I}|^{2}=\sum_{p}|{\cal I}_{p}|^{2}
. We can then conclude that
\mathbb{A}^{s}
(with
s\in\mathbb{N}
) is of the same form:

\mathbb{A}^{s}=\begin{pmatrix}f_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})[B]&g_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})[C]\\
g_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})[C^{\dagger}]&h_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})[D]\end{pmatrix}

where
f_{s},g_{s},h_{s}
denote some polynomial functions. One thus has

\mbox{Tr}[\mathbb{A}^{s}]=bf_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})|\mathcal{I}|^{2}+(d-a)h_{s}(|\mathcal{I}|^{2})\ ,

i.e., a function of only
|\mathcal{I}|^{2}
. Using the constraint on the cosmological constant
4.32
, we can then suppress the
|{\cal I}|^{2}
-dependency in the matrix. Since the eigenvalues can be written in terms of the invariants of the matrix (which are obtained from
\mbox{Tr}[\mathbb{A}^{s}]
), the mass states are thus not function of the intermediate energy scale
M_{2,p}
and
M_{3,p}
.

We can also show that
\mathcal{M}^{2}
can be expressed in a simpler form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}^{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a\mathbb{I}_{n-1}&0&0\\
0&a+b|{\cal I}|^{2}&c|{\cal I}|\\
0&\bar{c}|{\cal I}|&d\\
\end{pmatrix}

(4.46)

with the parameters
a,\ b,\ c
and
d
already defined in
Appendix F
. Since
a=|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}|m^{\prime}_{3/2}|^{2}=|m_{3/2}|^{2}
, the mass matrix leads to
(n-1)
fields with a mass equal to the gravitino mass. The two last states mix themselves through a submatrix equivalent to the case of only one singlet and one field from the hidden sector. The complete calculation to obtain the matrix structure
4.46
can be found in Appendix
F
, as well as the determination of the complete eigenvector basis using the Gram-Schmidt process.

4.3.4
Simple case: only one
S
-field

We now consider only one S field and one
\phi
field. The two relations
4.38
and
4.39
imply a dependency on the covariant derivative
\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}
. From the simplification
\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}\rangle=z^{\dagger}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}
(which corresponds to a decoupled contribution to the hidden sector and the matter sector in the superpotential
W_{0}
, or more generically
\partial_{a}\partial_{z}\omega_{0}=0
)
4
4

4

This is typical where the parameters in the superpotential
Eq. 4.19
are no longer function of the hidden sector (for example,
\lambda(z)=\lambda
).
, we obtain some information on the order of magnitude of some parameters. From
Eq. 4.38
, it follows from the inequality
M_{4}\ll m_{p}
(we assume real parameters):

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle|\ll|\langle 2w-z^{\dagger}\mathfrak{d}_{z}\bar{w}\rangle|\ll 1\ .

Moreover, using the relation
4.41
, we get from the equality
4.39
and the equality between
4.38
and
4.39
:

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle|\ll\langle\frac{|\mathfrak{d}_{z}w|}{\sqrt{|2w-z\mathfrak{d}_{z}w}}\rangle\ll 1\ ,

(4.47)

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle|\approx\frac{|\langle 2w-z\mathfrak{d}_{z}w\rangle|^{2}}{|\langle(\mathfrak{d}_{z}w)^{2}\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle|}|\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle|^{2}\sim\mathcal{O}(|\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle|^{2})\ .

(4.48)

In the case where we relax the condition
\partial_{a}\partial_{z}\omega_{0}=0
, the relations are more involved. We can solve the system of equations (
4.38
,
4.39
) as function of
\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}
, which gives:

\displaystyle\left<\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2\left<w\right>-\frac{m_{p}^{2}}{M_{4}^{2}}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>\right)\left<\frac{\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}}{\mathfrak{d}_{z}w}\right>,

(4.49)

\displaystyle\left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\sqrt{\left<\frac{6w^{3}+(\mathfrak{d}_{z}w)^{2}(\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w-4w)}{\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}}\right>}

(4.50)

(the sign in the notation
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
will be omitted when the sign of the solution is irrelevant for the discussion). The expression of
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle
can also be expressed as function of
\partial_{z}\partial_{a}\omega_{0}
:

\displaystyle\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}=\frac{1}{2c_{1}}\left(c_{2}^{2}-2c_{1}c_{3}\mp|c_{2}|\sqrt{c_{2}^{2}-4c_{1}c_{3}}\right)

(4.51)

where we introduce the coefficients
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
c_{3}
:

\displaystyle c_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<6w^{3}+(\mathfrak{d}_{z}w)^{2}(\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w-4w)\right>=\frac{\left<w^{3}\right>}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}}\left<6\xi_{3/2}^{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w}{w}-4\right>,

(4.52)

\displaystyle c_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<(\mathfrak{d}_{z}w)\ \partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>=\frac{\left<w\right>}{\xi_{3/2}}\left<\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>,

(4.53)

\displaystyle c_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<z\mathfrak{d}_{z}w-2w\right>\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}=\frac{\left<w\right>}{\xi_{3/2}}\left(\left<z\right>-2\xi_{3/2}\right)\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\ .

(4.54)

Note that the solution does not depend of the sign in
Eq. 4.50
. This configuration (with one matter field
\phi
) will be the main studied in this chapter. The two configurations (with and without the assumption
\partial_{a}\partial_{z}\omega_{0}=0
) will be analysed in the sequel.

A remark can be made on some limits of
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
. We have already mentioned that high values of
\xi_{3/2}
may be interesting to push-up the radiative corrections through the parameter
|{\cal I}|^{2}
. Taking the limit
\xi_{3/2}
to infinity gives

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\xi_{3/2}\rightarrow\infty}\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}=3\frac{m_{p}^{2}}{M_{4}^{4}}e^{-|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}|m_{3/2}|^{2}\ .

From
Eq. 4.32
, we see that the first and last term compensate each other leading to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\xi_{3/2}\rightarrow\infty}|{\cal I}|^{2}=0\ .

Taking high value of
\xi_{3/2}
may then not automatically leads to high loop corrections. The full numerical computation of the one-loop Higgs boson mass seems mandatory to understand the evolution regarding the
\xi_{3/2}
parameter (see Subsection
4.4.4
).

Assuming one singlet
S
, the mass matrix of the
z-S
sector is a simple
2\times 2
matrix. We can then easily obtain the mixing angle of
z-S
with the two eigenvalues denoted
m^{2}_{S^{\prime}}
(
S^{\prime}
being the lighter state) and
m^{2}_{\zeta^{\prime}}
(
\zeta^{\prime}
being the heavier state). Introducing

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{2}}\Big(\Big<\big|\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}-\big|\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}\Big>\Big)+2\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\prime}|^{2}-3\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}|^{2}-\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{a^{2}+4e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\Bigg(\left|2\overline{m}_{3/2}^{\prime\prime}\overline{\xi}_{3/2}-\overline{m}_{3/2}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\frac{\big|{\cal I}\big|^{2}}{m^{2}_{p}}\Bigg)}.

(4.55)

the mixing angle and the eigenvalues are then:

\displaystyle\cos\theta=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta-a}{2\Delta}}\ ,\sin\theta=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta+a}{2\Delta}}\ ,

(4.56)

and the eigenvalues

\displaystyle m^{2}_{\zeta^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg(e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{2}}\Big(\Big<\big|\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}(1-8|\xi_{\frac{3}{2}}|^{2})+\big|\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}\Big>\Big)+\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}\big|^{2}(24|\xi_{\frac{3}{2}}|^{2}-9)+\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}

(4.57)

\displaystyle\pm\Bigg\{\Bigg(e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{2}}\Big(\Big<\big|\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}-\big|\mathfrak{d}_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\big|^{2}\Big>\Big)+\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\prime}|^{2}(2-3\big|\xi_{\frac{3}{2}}\big|^{2})-\big|m_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+4e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\Bigg(\left|2\overline{m}_{3/2}^{\prime\prime}\overline{\xi}_{3/2}-\overline{m}_{3/2}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\frac{\big|{\cal I}\big|^{2}}{m^{2}_{p}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}^{1/2}\Bigg)\ .

The eigenstates reduce then to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} S^{\prime}&=&\cos\theta\;S-\varepsilon m_{p}\sin\theta\;z\ ,\\
z^{\prime}&=&\sin\theta\;S+\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{p}}\cos\theta\;z\end{aligned}\ \quad\text{with}\quad\varepsilon=\frac{\Big|\Big<\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S\partial z^{\dagger}}\Big>\Big|}{\Big<\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S\partial z^{\dagger}}\Big>}\ .

(4.58)

We have derived all the interesting quantities for our investigation.

4.4
Order of magnitude of the effects from the hybrid sector

In the previous section, we have investigated the form of the constraints coming from the nullity of the cosmological constant and the minimisation of the potential. The mass matrix has also been calculated in the general case of
n
singlets. We now discuss the loop effects coming from the hybrid sector.

Following the results
4.57
and
4.58
, the state
S
is described by the two mass eigenstates
S^{\prime}
and
\zeta^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle S=\zeta^{\prime}\sin\theta+S^{\prime}\cos\theta\ .

Since all the one-loop contributions (see
Eq. 4.28
and
Eq. 4.29
) can be rewritten in the general form
SS^{\dagger}O
with
O
a specific operator, then the one-loop (
S
) contribution is:

\delta O\sim\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}(m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta+m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)

(4.59)

Assuming that the heaviest sector is the main contribution to the correction, we can first analyse the contribution
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta
. Those studies will draw attention to the relevant regimes which allow a Higgs boson mass near
125\ \mathrm{GeV}
. After this discussion, a more complete numerical computation of the possible loop-effect on the Higgs boson mass is done.

4.4.1
Set of constraints on the model

Before making a numerical analysis, we must consider all the constraints that prevent unphysical points. Our parameters-space is an eight-dimensional space:
\{m_{3/2},\langle z\rangle,\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle,\xi_{3/2},M_{4},\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle,sgn(\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle)\rangle,s\}
(with
s
the sign present in the solution of
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle

Eq. 4.51
). We assume all real parameters. Note also that we have freedom on the sign of the parameters
\{m_{3/2},\langle z\rangle,\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w,\xi_{3/2},\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle\}
. This parameters-space is already constrained by the previous results in
4.3.2
.

The vanishing of the cosmological constant express the parameter
|{\cal I}|^{2}
as a function of the other parameters through the relation
4.32
. So we must take care of the sign of the parameter
|{\cal I}|^{2}
. The choice of parameters must then fulfilled the following inequality

\displaystyle|{\cal I}|^{2}\geq 0

(4.60)

which can be rewritten in the following form (using the relation
4.50
)

\displaystyle|{\cal I}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{4}^{4}\langle w\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-1)}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}}\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}-\frac{\left(2\langle w\rangle(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)+\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle\right)}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>}\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{4}}\right)\geq 0\ .

(4.61)

Moreover, the calculation of the mass spectrum (
i.e.
,
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}
and
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}
) could leads to negative squared masses. To recover a correct spectrum, we must impose:

\displaystyle m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}\geq 0\ ,\quad m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\geq 0\ .

The two last constraints come from
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle
solution’s in
Eq. 4.51
. Writing the equality of
Eq. 4.49
and
Eq. 4.50
(by expressing
\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}
as function of the other parameters), we get:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{c_{1}}{\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>}}=\mp\frac{c_{2}}{c_{3}+\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>}

(4.62)

where the sign in the right term comes from the sign in
4.50
. From those relations, we get:

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}}{\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}}\geq 0,

(4.63)

\displaystyle\frac{c_{2}}{c_{3}+\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}}\leq 0,\geq 0\ \text{for}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>\geq 0,\leq 0\ ,

(4.64)

meaning that not all couples of solutions
(\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm},\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\mp})
are possible. By using the solution
4.51
in those inequalities, we identify the region which are valid regarding the equation
4.62
. We obtain:

\displaystyle i)\

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{3}<0:

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{2}<0\Leftrightarrow\ \rm{the\ only\ solution\ is}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}(\rm{resp.}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+})\ \rm{if}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right><0\

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\rm{resp.}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>>0)

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{2}>0\Leftrightarrow\ \rm{the\ only\ solution\ is}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}(\rm{resp.}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+})\ \rm{if}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>>0\

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\rm{resp.}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right><0)

(4.65)

\displaystyle ii)\

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{3}>0:

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{2}<0\land c_{2}^{2}-4c_{1}c_{3}>0\Leftrightarrow\ \rm{the\ two\ solutions}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}\ \rm{are\ valid\ when}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>>0

\displaystyle c_{1}c_{2}>0\land c_{2}^{2}-4c_{1}c_{3}>0\Leftrightarrow\ \rm{the\ two\ solutions}\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}\ \rm{are\ valid\ when}\ \left<\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right><0

Note that in the case where
\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}=0
, the coefficient
c_{2}
drop out of the equations. There is then only one solution for
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle
and the only constrain is
4.63
which can be rewritten as
c_{1}c_{3}<0
.

From the definitions of the coefficients
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
c_{3}
in
4.53
, assuming
\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle>4\langle w\rangle
(which is in accordance with the assumptions
4.41
and
4.25
), the sign of
c_{1}
is then equal to the sign of
w
, and so
m_{3/2}
. Thus:

\displaystyle\text{sgn}(c_{1}c_{3})=\text{sgn}(\xi_{3/2}(\langle z\rangle-2\xi_{3/2}))\ \text{and}\ \text{sgn}(c_{1}c_{2})=\text{sgn}(\xi_{3/2}\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle)\ .

(4.66)

We have already a restriction on the possible values of
\xi_{3/2}
which comes from the fine-tuning of the cosmological constant (
|\xi_{3/2}|>\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
in
4.32
). Since we have by definition
\langle z\rangle=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we are mostly in the case
\text{sgn}(c_{1}c_{3})<0
(independent of
\text{sgn}(\xi_{3/2})
and
\text{sgn}(\langle z\rangle)
) as long as the value of
\xi_{3/2}
is outside the domain
[\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\frac{|\langle z\rangle|}{2}]\cup[-\frac{|\langle z\rangle|}{2},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}]
, which is generally small or closed.

Note also that the relation
4.63
can be rewritten as

\frac{\left(2\langle w\rangle(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)+\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle\right)}{\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>}\geq 0\ .

(4.67)

Since this positive term is subtracted in
Eq. 4.61
, if its contribution is not subleading (
i.e.
,
\left|\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>\right|
is relatively small), the configurations could become highly constrained.

We can now compute the order of magnitude of the correction
Eq. 4.59
.

4.4.2
A first approach:
\partial_{a}\partial_{z}\omega_{0}=0

We assume the simplifying assumption
\langle\partial_{\phi}\partial_{z}\omega_{0}\rangle=0
. Starting from
m_{\zeta}^{2}
and
\sin\theta
in
Eq. 4.57
and
Eq. 4.56
, we can substitute
|{\cal I}|^{2}
and
\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}
using the vanishing of the cosmological constant
4.32
and the minimisation equation along the field
z

4.39
. After lengthy computation and Taylor expansion with the gravitino mass
m_{3/2}
we obtain (we assume real
v.e.vs
for the fields):

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\frac{e^{-\frac{\langle z\rangle^{2}}{2}}\xi_{3/2}}{(\langle z\rangle-2\xi_{3/2})\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}\frac{m_{3/2}m_{p}^{2}}{M_{4}^{3}}+{\cal O}(m_{3/2}^{2}),

(4.68)

\displaystyle m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\langle z\rangle^{2}}\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle^{2}\frac{M_{4}^{6}}{m_{p}^{4}}+{\cal O}(m_{3/2})\ .

(4.69)

which lead to

m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta=4\,\frac{e^{\frac{\langle z\rangle^{2}}{2}}\xi_{3/2}\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{(\langle z\rangle-2\xi_{3/2})}\frac{m_{3/2}M_{4}^{3}}{m_{p}^{2}}+{\cal O}(m_{3/2}^{2})

(4.70)

(we denote here
m_{3/2}\equiv|m_{3/2}|>0
and
\xi_{3/2}\equiv|\xi_{3/2}|>1/\sqrt{3}
). This relation requires for consistency the inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{(\langle z\rangle-2\xi_{3/2})}>0

which is not in contradiction with the constraint
|{\cal I}|^{2}>0
.

Supposing a energy scale in the matter sector of
M_{EW}\approx 10^{2}\ \text{GeV}
we see that the correction is relatively small (
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\ \propto\ 10^{-32}m_{3/2}
with
m_{p}=10^{18}\ \mathrm{GeV}
). To consider large loop-effects, we must then assume models with several energy scales in the matter sector, such as GUT theories with
M_{4}=M_{GUT}\approx 10^{15}\ \text{GeV}
. In this manner, the value of
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}
mass increase and gives
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\ \propto\ 10^{7}m_{3/2}
. The superpotential can then be written as:

\displaystyle\langle W_{0}(z,S,\phi)\rangle=M_{4}^{3}\langle\omega_{0}(z,S,\phi)\rangle=M_{4}^{3}\langle\omega_{0}(z,S,\phi_{GUT},\phi_{EW})\rangle

with
\langle\phi_{GUT}\rangle\approx\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\langle\phi_{EW}\rangle\ll 1
(since those fields are rescaled with a GUT scale). Notice that, even we assume only one singlet
S
(and so no
\mathcal{U}
-field in
\omega_{0}
), we will estimate the order of magnitude of the one-loop effect
4.29
(as well as
4.28
) in Section
4.4.4
.

4.4.3
A first approach: Analysing several regimes

If we relax the assumption on
\langle\partial_{z}\partial\phi\omega_{0}\rangle
, the relations are more complex. One finds after using the relations
4.32
,
4.49
and
4.50
:

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\frac{e^{-\langle z\rangle^{2}}}{\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}\frac{m_{3/2}^{2}}{M_{4}^{2}}\frac{\Upsilon}{1+\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle}\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}}+{\cal O}(m_{3/2}^{3})

(4.71)

where

\Upsilon=\frac{3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-1-\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle}\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}}{1+\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle}\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}}.

(4.72)

and

\displaystyle m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\langle z\rangle^{2}}\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{2}}\,\left(\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle+\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\right)+{\cal O}(m_{3/2})\ .

(4.73)

Thus:

\displaystyle m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\frac{\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle}\frac{m_{3/2}^{2}M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\Upsilon+{\cal O}(m_{3/2}^{3})

(4.74)

Contrary to the simplifying case (where
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\sim m_{3/2}M_{4}^{3}/m_{p}^{2}
), we see that the loop-effect turns to be
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\sim m_{3/2}^{2}M_{4}^{2}/m_{p}^{2}
. A
M_{4}
prefactor has been replaced by
m_{3/2}
, which seems to reduce the effect in the loop. The parameter
\Upsilon

Eq. 4.72
can however push-up the correction in the case of high values of
\xi_{3/2}
.

Note that is we tend smoothly to
\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle=0
, the two solutions
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle
are degenerated and gives back the result
Eq. 4.70
with:

\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}=-c_{3}=\frac{\left<w\right>}{\xi_{3/2}}\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\left<z\right>\right)\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}.

(4.75)

The study of the leading behavior of the correction in the limit
\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle\rightarrow 0
is however not so simple. Indeed, since we need
\langle w\rangle\ll 1
and considering the expression
4.52
,
4.53
and
4.54
, we must consider the ratio

\displaystyle\rho=\frac{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle}{\langle w\rangle}\ .

Three regimes are then studied,
\rho^{-1}\ll 1
,
\rho\ll 1
and
\rho={\cal O}(1)
.

Regime
\mathbf{\rho^{-1}\ll 1}
:

After expanding
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}

and
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+}
around
\rho^{-1}=0
in powers of
\left<w\right>
(with
\left<w\right>\ll\min[1,\left<\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>]
), one finds

\displaystyle\left.\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}\right|_{(\rho^{-1}\ll 1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle w\rangle^{2}\frac{(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle)^{2}}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}}\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{4}}+{\cal O}(\langle w\rangle^{3}),

(4.76)

\displaystyle\left.\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+}\right|_{(\rho^{-1}\ll 1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}+2\langle w\rangle\left(\left(2-3\xi_{3/2}^{2}\right)\frac{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle^{2}}+\frac{\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle\right)}{\xi_{3/2}}\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\right)

(4.77)

\displaystyle+{\cal O}(\langle w\rangle^{2}).

The solutions are totally decoupled. Obviously, these expressions are not valid for
\left<\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>\to 0
(which is clearly the case in the leading term of
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}
, while the singularity of
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+}
appears in higher orders not shown here).

We now tend to analyse the order of magnitude of
4.74
, assuming the two solutions
Eq. 4.76
and
Eq. 4.76
.

We first consider the solution
Eq. 4.76
since it seems to be the only configuration that leads to an enhancement of
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta
. Indeed, the leading term of
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{-}
being proportional to
\langle w^{2}\rangle
, the
m_{3/2}^{2}
factor in
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta
is cancels out and can possibly increase the magnitude of the loop. Nevertheless, the first order contribution in
4.76
cancels the
\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{4}}
suppression in
4.61
, which make the subtracted positive
term important and potentially leads to
|{\cal I}|^{2}<0
for most values of parameters. One can recast the two equations (
4.61
) and (
4.67
)
respectively in a simple form

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &&A\langle w\rangle^{2}-(B\langle w\rangle+C)\geq 0,\\
&&B\langle w\rangle+C\geq 0,\end{aligned}\quad\text{with :}\quad\begin{aligned} &A=M_{4}^{2}\frac{(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-1)(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle)^{2}}{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}},&\\
&B=2m_{p}^{2}\frac{\xi_{3/2}^{2}(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)}{\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle},&\\
&C=m_{p}^{2}\xi_{3/2}^{2}.&\end{aligned}

(4.78)

The coefficient
B
can be positive or negative while
C
and
A
are defined positive (since
|\xi_{3/2}|>\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
). Recalling that
|\langle w\rangle|\ll 1
we identify two possible ranges of values for
\langle w\rangle
5
5

5

The two other solutions, namely
\langle w\rangle>\frac{B+\sqrt{B^{2}+4AC}}{2A}
when

B>0
and
\langle w\rangle<\frac{B-\sqrt{B^{2}+4AC}}{2A}
when
B<0
lead to high values of
\langle w\rangle\gg 1
and so are not considered here.
:

\displaystyle\Big(B>0\;\text{and}\;-\frac{C}{B}<

\displaystyle\langle w\rangle

\displaystyle<\frac{B-\sqrt{B^{2}+4AC}}{2A}\Big)

(4.79)

or

\displaystyle\Big(B<0\;\text{and}\;\frac{B+\sqrt{B^{2}+4AC}}{2A}<

\displaystyle\langle w\rangle

\displaystyle<-\frac{C}{B}\Big)

(4.80)

which corresponds to a width
\langle\Delta w\rangle
of the allowed interval of

\displaystyle\langle\Delta w\rangle=\sgn(B)\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{8m_{p}^{2}}\frac{(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-1)(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle)^{2}\langle{\mathfrak{d}}_{z}^{2}w\rangle^{3}}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)^{3}\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}+{\cal O}(\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{4}})\ .

(4.81)

The range of values for
\langle w\rangle
seems then extremely small for relatively high values of
\xi_{3/2}
,
i.e.
, the solution is highly fine-tuned. A possible solution to increase the range
\langle\Delta w\rangle
can be to choose
\xi_{3/2}^{2}\approx 2/3
. However, this value drives the intervals outside the condition
|\langle w\rangle|\ll 1
. We thus conclude that the regime given by Eq. (
4.76
) is actually not viable.

Considering now the solution
Eq. 4.77
, we see that the
\frac{M_{4}^{4}}{m_{p}^{4}}
term in
Eq. 4.61
is not reduced by
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle
. The positivity of
|{\cal I}|^{2}
is generally always satisfied, leaving to the only constraint
Eq. 4.63
, which is relatively easy to achieve. However, due to the non-dependency of the leading term of
Eq. 4.77
in
\langle w\rangle
, the effect still remains suppressed by the
m_{3/2}^{2}
factor in
Eq. 4.74
. The regime
\rho^{-1}\ll 1
seems then not appropriate to generate huge loop effects.

Regime
\mathbf{\rho\ll 1}
and
\mathbf{\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)}
:

We now consider the two other regimes, namely
\rho\ll 1
and
\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)
. The expansion of
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{-}

and
\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{+}
in the two mentioned limits gives:

\displaystyle\left.\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}\right|_{\rho\ll 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left<w\right>}{\xi_{3/2}}\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\left<z\right>\right)\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\mp\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle\sqrt{\frac{\left<w\right>\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle\right)}{\xi_{3/2}\left(\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle+(6\xi_{3/2}^{2}-4)\left<w\right>\right)}}\frac{M_{4}}{m_{p}}

(4.82)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle+(6\xi_{3/2}^{2}-4)\left<w\right>}+{\cal O}(\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{3}),

and

\displaystyle\left.\left<\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\right>_{\mp}\right|_{\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left<w\right>}{\xi_{3/2}}\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\left<z\right>\right)\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}\mp\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle\sqrt{\frac{\left<w\right>\left(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle\right)}{\xi_{3/2}\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}}\frac{M_{4}}{m_{p}}

(4.83)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}+{\cal O}(\langle w\rangle^{3},(\rho-1)^{3}),

The two leading orders reproduce the result of
Eq. 4.75
in the case
\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle=0
, meaning that the loop effects can be sizable. Note also that the expansion
Eq. 4.82
is not
valid in the limit
\left<w\right>\to 0
.
6
6

6

In fact, divergences in the limit
\left<w\right>\to 0
appears in the terms of order
\langle\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{3}
and higher.
A difference between
\mathbf{\rho^{-1}\ll 1}
and the solutions
\mathbf{\rho\ll 1}
and
\mathbf{\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)}
can be noted. While
Eq. 4.76
and
Eq. 4.77
) are valid for
\langle w\rangle\ll 1
, the relations
4.82
and
4.83
) impose the condition
|\left<\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>|\ll\frac{M_{4}}{m_{p}}
. This is due to the decreasing powers of
\frac{M_{4}}{m_{p}}
in the terms with increasing powers of
\left<\partial_{z}\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\right>
as can be seen from
Eq. 4.82
and
Eq. 4.83
.

In both cases (
\mathbf{\rho\ll 1}
and
\mathbf{\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)}
), the negative term in
Eq. 4.61
is now of order
\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{m_{p}^{2}}
and now function of
\langle w\rangle
as:

\displaystyle|{\cal I}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{4}^{6}}{m_{p}^{2}}\langle w\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-1)}{\xi_{3/2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\xi_{3/2}(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle)}\left(2(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)+\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle w\rangle}\right)\right)\geq 0\ .

(4.84)

We also have

\frac{1}{\xi_{3/2}(2\xi_{3/2}-\langle z\rangle)}\left(2(3\xi_{3/2}^{2}-2)+\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle w\rangle}\right)\geq 0\ .

(4.85)

The last condition is essentially always satisfied if
\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w/w\rangle>4
and the fact that
\sgn(c_{1}c_{3})<0
(see the discussion in
4.4.1
). However,
|{\cal I}|^{2}
is not necessarily positive. By developing the relation
4.84
, we note that some the parameters-space zone are forbidden. For example, taking
\xi_{3/2}
,
\langle z\rangle
and
\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle w\rangle}
positive automatically lead to
|{\cal I}|^{2}<0
. Numerical investigations reveal four different regions described by
\text{sgn}(\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle w\rangle})=\pm 1
and
\text{sgn}(z\xi_{3/2})=\pm 1
(
i.e.
, all the regions with the sign of
\xi_{3/2}
and
\langle z\rangle
swapped are equivalent). The different regions are shown in
Figure 4.2
.

Figure 4.2
:
Allowed region by the constraints
Eq. 4.84
and
Eq. 4.85
as function of
\log_{10}(\xi_{3/2})
and
\log_{10}(\frac{\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle}{\langle w\rangle})
. Upper-left: the inequality
|{\cal I}|^{2}\geq 0
is not satisfied in all the plane. The red color is the superposition of the two regimes.

The regimes
\rho\sim\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\rho\ll 1
seem to be more interesting for high corrections from the
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta
contribution. However, since the prefactor in
Eq. 4.28
and
Eq. 4.29
can be sizable, the regime
\rho\gg 1
will also be considered in our numerical analysis. Note that for simplicity, we denote the contribution
\phi^{\dagger}\phi S^{\dagger}S
as
purely
hard and the
\mathcal{U}S^{\dagger}H_{U}\cdot H_{D}
as the
superpotential
contribution.

4.4.4
Numerical computation of the order of magnitude of the corrections

For our numerical study, we assume that the
v.e.v
of the field from the hidden sector is
|\langle z\rangle|=1
as well as the parameter
|\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle|=1
(see
Eq. 4.41
). We also set
M_{4}=10^{15}\ \mathrm{GeV}
and
m_{p}=2\times 10^{18}\ \mathrm{GeV}
. The analysis is done for several values of the parameter
\rho
:

\displaystyle\{\rho\}=\{0,10^{-2},-10^{-2},1,-1,10^{2},-10^{2}\}

and by varying the gravitino mass in the range
[10^{-3}\ \mathrm{GeV},10^{3}\ \mathrm{GeV}]
. All the possibles configurations for the signs of the parameters are investigated. All solutions regarding the choice of
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
and
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
are also taken into account. The complete contribution of the two terms
Eq. 4.28
and
Eq. 4.29
on the Higgs boson mass is written as:

\displaystyle m_{h}^{(1L)}=\sqrt{m_{h}^{2}{}^{(TL)}+\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}m_{p}^{2}}e^{|\langle z\rangle|^{2}}\Big(m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta+m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\Big)\Big(\sqrt{|{\cal I}|^{2}}+\frac{1}{m_{p}^{2}}\big(4|\xi_{3/2}|^{2}-2\big)|{\cal I}|^{2}\Big)}

(4.86)

with
m_{h}^{(TL)}
, the Higgs boson mass containing the tree-level and one-loop contributions coming from the soft-breaking terms, assumed to be
115\ \mathrm{GeV}
. In a first analysis, even though the correction
Eq. 4.29
corresponds to the off-diagonal matrix element, we assume that this term directly contributes to the Higgs boson mass without first diagonalising the matrix. This simplification is done considering the known effect for singlet-extension of the MSSM (see for example
Eq. 5.2
). A more proper method will be to diagonalise the corrected mass matrix and compute the eigenvalues of the matrix.

We first make a remark on the sign of the parameters. Analysing all the previous equations, we see that switching the sign of all the parameters

\displaystyle\{p_{i}\}=\{\xi_{3/2},\langle z\rangle,\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle,m_{3/2},\rho\}\rightarrow\{-p_{i}\}=\{-\xi_{3/2},-\langle z\rangle,-\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle,-m_{3/2},-\rho\}

leads to equivalent results. This reduces the number of configurations to investigate.

We consider in a first step the case when
\rho=0
. In this case, we recall that the two solutions
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
are degenerated. Also, the constraint
Eq. 4.64
is not appropriate since
c_{2}=0
. However, many configurations lead to no possible solutions in the range of values defined above. All the space is generally forbidden by the other constraints in Section
4.4.1
. The positivity of the lighter state
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}
seems to be the most restrictive condition. Only a few configurations are possible which can be separated into two different cases.

\log_{10}\big(|{\cal I}|^{2}\big)

(b)

Evolution of
\log_{10}\big(m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}\big)

(c)

Evolution of
\big(m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}-m_{\zeta_{0}^{\prime}}^{2}\big)/m_{\zeta_{0}^{\prime}}^{2}
with
m_{\zeta_{0}^{\prime}}^{2}=10^{17.23017}\text{GeV}^{2}

(d)

Evolution of
\log_{10}\big(\sin^{2}\theta\big)

:
Evolution of several parameters as function of
\log_{10}\big(|m_{3/2}/(1\ \mathrm{GeV})|\big)
and
\log_{10}\big(|\xi_{3/2}|\big)
for
\mathrm{sgn}(\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle)=\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{3/2})=\langle z\rangle=-1
,
\rho=0
and
\mathrm{sgn}(m_{3/2})=1
.

The configuration
\mathrm{sgn}(\langle z\rangle)=\mathrm{sgn}(\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle)=\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{3/2})=-1
and
\mathrm{sgn}(m_{3/2})=1
(see
Figure 4.3
and
Figure 4.4
) is quite representative to the first part of the solutions. The light state
4.3(b)
has a mass between the TeV scale and the MeV while the heavier one
4.3(c)
remain at
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}\sim 10^{17}\ \mathrm{GeV}^{2}
. The computation of the mixing angle
4.3(d)
shows us that the light state
S^{\prime}
is mainly singlet. Finally, the magnitude of
|{\cal I}|^{2}
in
4.3(a)
allows to determinine the order of magnitude of each contribution in
Eq. 4.86
. Taking the highest possible value of
|{\cal I}|^{2}\approx 10^{40}\ \mathrm{GeV}^{2}
, the factor
\sqrt{|{\cal I}|^{2}}/m_{p}^{2}
coming from the contribution
Eq. 4.29
is approximately
10^{-16}
while the second contribution gives
|{\cal I}\xi_{3/2}|^{2}/m_{p}^{4}\approx 10^{-32}
. Taking the highest value of
\xi_{3/2}
to boost the second contribution, we obtain respectively for the two factors
10^{-26}
and
10^{-22}
. Even though the purely hard contribution is suppressed in
m_{p}^{4}
, high values of
\xi_{3/2}
can then push this loop effect and exceed the
superpotential
contribution. However, as we can see in all the figures, increasing the values of
\xi_{3/2}
must be coordinated with a reduction of the gravitino mass to pass the constraints. This leads to lower the mass
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}
and the parameters
\sin^{2}\theta
and
|{\cal I}|^{2}
which reduce the loop effects. Moreover, taking too high values of
\xi_{3/2}
is non-natural and generate a fine-tuning. The one-loop Higgs Boson mass is shown in Figure
4.4(a)
. As we can see from this evolution and the previous discussion, the effects are mostly nonexistent. Finally, note that the restriction for
\log_{10}|\xi_{3/2}|<6
is coming from the negativity of the squared mass
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}
where the constraint for high
\xi_{3/2}
values corresponds to both
|{\cal I}|^{2}<0
and
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}<0
.

(b)

:

as function of

and
\log_{10}\big(|\xi_{3/2}|\big)
for two configurations.

(a)

Evolution of

:
Evolution of several parameters as function of

and

for

,

,

,

and
\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{3/2})=1
.

A second type of solution has been found. A representative configuration is
\mathrm{sgn}(\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle)=\mathrm{sgn}(\langle z\rangle)=-1
and
\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{3/2})=\mathrm{sgn}(m_{3/2})=1
(see
4.4(b)
and
Figure 4.5
). High values of
\xi_{3/2}
are not forbidden for fixed
m_{3/2}
compared to the other solutions. The region with low
\xi_{3/2}
values is constrained by the negativity of the lightest state’s squared mass. The evolution of the various parameters regarding
m_{3/2}
and
\xi_{3/2}
are also quite different. While the mass of the heaviest state remains unchanged in the
(\log_{10}(\xi_{3/2}),\log_{10}(m_{3/2}/(1\ \mathrm{GeV}))
-plane in the previous solution, high
\xi_{3/2}
values lead to quasi non-degenerate states
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}\approx m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}
. The mixing angle is also pushed to
\sin^{2}\theta\approx\frac{1}{2}
which maximise the mixing between the two states. The full one-loop Higgs boson mass is shown in
4.4(b)
. Due to the possibility of taking high
\xi_{3/2}
values, the correction coming from the pure hard term increases intensively, leading to a really high Higgs mass, meaning that possible solutions exist for obtaining
m_{h}\approx 125\ \mathrm{GeV}
. Nonetheless, assuming
m_{3/2}=1\ \mathrm{TeV}
as the maximum value for the gravitino mass, those effects only appear for
\xi_{3/2}>10^{14}
which is non-natural.

We can study the variation of the full correction depending on the possible values of
\langle z\rangle
and
\langle\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}w\rangle
. We take the gravitino mass as
m_{3/2}=1\ \mathrm{TeV}
to increase the loop contributions and
\xi_{3/2}=10^{5}
to pass all the constraints (following results from
4.4(a)
). The variation of
m_{h}^{(1L)}
considering the same configuration as
Figure 4.3
for the inputs is shown in
Figure 4.6
. We notice that the loop corrections still remain low for values of
|\langle z\rangle|
close to
1
. However, the mass increase exponentially when
\langle z\rangle>4
, leading to possible configurations with lower value of
\xi_{3/2}
as in the case of
4.4(b)
. This is simply due to the presence of the
v.e.vs
from the hidden sector in the exponential in
Eq. 4.28
and
Eq. 4.29
, and more generally in the scalar potential. This value of
\langle z\rangle
can be seen as non-natural, but may be legitimate for more complex models (see Subsection
4.5
).

Figure 4.6
:
Evolution of
m_{h}^{(1L)}
as function of

and

for

,

and
m_{3/2}=10^{3}\ \mathrm{GeV}
. The white region is forbidden due to the restriction
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}>0
.

We now analyse the general case
\rho\neq 0
. The two solutions
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
correspond in this case to two different results. The choice of
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{+}
or
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{-}
leads to the same numerical results (since this parameter always appears as
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle^{2}
), but to different constraints following
Eq. 4.64
. Note that the degeneracy of the solutions
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
has been obtained for
\rho=0
but can also be obtained with the more general assumption
c_{2}\rightarrow 0
(with
c_{2}
in
Eq. 4.53
) which is also true for
\xi_{3/2}\rightarrow\infty
,
i.e.
, the two solutions
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
are equivalent for high
\xi_{3/2}
values.

For all the values of
\rho=\{\pm 10^{2},\pm 1,\pm 10^{-2}\}
, we have found the same types of evolution than for the case
\rho=0
. A lot of configurations lead to inconsistency with the constraints in Subsection
4.4.1
. The distributions for several configurations representative to the general results can be found in
Figure 4.7
for the solution
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{+}
and in
Figure 4.8
for
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{-}
.

First considering the
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{+}
solutions. As already said in our first qualitative analysis (see
subsection 4.4.3
), the solutions for
\rho=\pm 1
and
\pm 10^{-2}
must be quite equivalent to the case
\rho=0
. The numerical results are quite in accordance with those statements. Only some differences appear at low value of
\log_{10}\xi_{3/2}
. Possible configurations exists only for high values of
\xi_{3/2}
. Those effects results from the same mechanisms as
Figure 4.5
and so are non-natural.

Even though our qualitative analysis for
\rho\geq 1
with
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{+}
have pointed out possible difficulties for high corrections, we can numerically find possible configurations for high
\xi_{3/2}
values (see
4.7(a)
or
4.7(b)
). However, those configurations are either highly fine-tuned or do not push the Higgs boson mass to
125\ \mathrm{GeV}
.

(a)

and using the solution

.

We consider now the solution
\langle\partial_{\phi}^{2}\omega_{0}\rangle_{-}
. Our analytic investigation in Subsection
subsection 4.4.3
showed a high fine-tuning on the gravitino mass for high values of the parameter
\rho
. The same result has been obtained in the numerical investigation. Indeed, no configurations for
\rho=\pm 10^{2}
have passed the imposed restrictions. In the case of
\rho=\{\pm 1,\pm 10^{-2}\}
, for a specific choice of
\mathrm{sgn}(\rho)
and
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{\pm}
, only a quarter of the configurations gives possible results. When a solution with
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{+}
pass the constraints, the equivalent configuration with
\langle\partial_{\phi}\omega_{0}\rangle_{-}
is completely forbidden (and
vice versa
). Possible solutions are presented in
Figure 4.8
. In the same manner as before, no interesting zone of the parameters-space has been discovered to increase the Higgs boson mass. The evolution of
|{\cal I}|^{2}
,
\sin^{2}\theta
and
m_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{2}
are equivalent to the case
\rho=0
with
Figure 4.3
. The only difference comes from the lightest state
m_{S^{\prime}}^{2}
which remains higher than the
\mathrm{GeV}
scale.

.

\langle z\rangle

, we also remark possible benchmarks which conduct to
m_{h}^{(1L)}\approx 125\ \mathrm{GeV}
for

(see

). These solutions will be discussed in the next section
4.5
.
